South Korea Faces Consequences of Tough Anti-Bullying Policy

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Education  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 3, 2026 | Reviewed by Tyler Woods

It’s been 27 years—over a quarter century--since the Columbine High School massacre ignited the worldwide war against bullying. Yet many nations that enthusiastically embrace this war discover that bullying becomes a more serious problem.

South Korea is one such country. It has made bullying a major cultural issue, producing a large number of popular “K-dramas” (Korean TV series) depicting the brutality of bullying, including the recent Netflix hit, Teach You a Lesson. Despite this massive anti-bullying activism (or perhaps because of it?), a 2025 survey found that South Korea's violence victimization rate in elementary school hit a record high.

A basic tenet of antibullyism is that bullying must not be tolerated and needs to be met with tough and consistent punishments, or “consequences.” Make the price of bullying too high, it is assumed, and any bully’s appetite to cause people pain will evaporate. Although it has become widely recognized that punishment is not an effective response to bullying,1 the belief in punishment continues unimpeded, probably because it seems intuitively correct to the average person, and perhaps because nothing else bullying researchers have presented seems to work reliably.

Thus, South Korea has adopted what sounds like a tough but logical consequence for bullying. Students with bullying on their records are disqualified from acceptance into public universities. This punishment causes a huge financial loss for students and can seriously impact their future. Given the importance Koreans place on higher education, certainly such a policy must be an effective deterrence to bullying, right?

Not if we consider human nature. Let’s say you get a traffic ticket. When will you fight it harder? When the fine is $50, with no points against your driver's license, or $50,000, and your license gets suspended? Well, the same thing is true if the huge punishments are for bullying violations, as recent news stories from South Korea confirm.

Before we continue, it's essential to recognize that the bullying that typically occurs in Korean schools does not resemble the bloodcurdling cruelty that is depicted in the K-dramas. Such behavior should result in incarceration. As in many other advanced nations, bullying in reality consists of any behaviors that can cause someone to get upset.

Students don’t suddenly become nicer because anti-bullying policies threaten them with severe educational penalties. Instead, they—their parents, really—fight back. An increasingly popular way is to countersue the educational system. As the title of one article in the Korean JoongAng Daily informs us, As consequences of bullying records grow, perpetrators turn to countersuits for leverage. It reveals the nightmare unleashed by these Korean laws. These lawsuits typically last years and cost tens of thousands of dollars.

To help the cover the expense, a growing number of parents in Korea are purchasing bullying insurance policies, as described in another article titled, As school violence becomes legal issue, insurers cash in. :

The number of insurance payouts related to school violence among five major nonlife insurers… surged from 231 cases in 2021 to 1,297 in 2022, 3,388 in 2023 and 3,443 in 2025. This represents a nearly fifteenfold rise in just five years.... The increasing pace has accelerated further this year, with 2,056 cases already recorded in January and February alone.

Furthermore, these lawsuits are being filed even for the most minor of bullying complaints. The Korea JoongAng article presents one parent’s experience with a situation that hardly meets the criteria of bullying:

One parent…started looking into a school violence insurance program after their fifth-grader was mired in a 25 million won ($16,580) lawsuit for repeatedly asking to borrow items and was accused of “having a look” at another student in their school.

The local education office concluded that no action was necessary, but the other student’s parent did not accept the decision and filed a damage lawsuit. The court dismissed the case in full last month.

After going through both the committee process and a civil lawsuit, the parent faced significant psychological and financial burdens, leading them to seek insurance coverage.

The purpose of anti-bullying laws is to reduce the misery caused by bullying. In South Korea, however, the misery of bullying is being dwarfed by the financial expense and psychological pain inflicted by prolonged, expensive lawsuits.

1. American Psychological Association. (2006, August 9). Zero tolerance policies are not as effective as thought, APA task force finds. https://www.apa.org/news/press/releases/2006/08/zero-tolerance

Commanders place Daron Payne, Javon Kinlaw, Jeremy McNichols on IR

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Commanders have placed defensive lineman Daron Payne, defensive lineman Javon Kinlaw and running back Jeremy McNichols on injured reserve, the team announced Tuesday.

Payne will miss the rest of the season with a season-ending foot injury.

Kinlaw will need extended rehab to heal a lingering knee injury.

Kinlaw and McNichols will miss at least the next four games before returning.

The Commanders have Tim Settle and Shy Tuttle on the interior of their defense, with Jer’Zhan Newton managing a pectoral injury.

The team claimed running back Robert Henry Jr. off waivers from Seattle to help replace McNichols. Henry was previously with the Commanders before the Seahawks signed him off Washington’s practice squad on Oct. 6.

An undrafted rookie out of UTSA, Henry has not yet appeared in a regular-season game.

NTSB says crucial plane parts found three miles from Florida crash site

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The NTSB found "the majority" of the Learjet's left elevator - a control surface located on the tail - in three pieces, NTSB member Tom Chapman said

Crucial components of a private jet that crashed in Palm Beach, Florida - including a part that controlled the up-and-down movements of the nose of the plane - were found 1.3 (2 km) to 3.8 miles away from the crash site, an investigator said on Tuesday.

National Transportation Safety Board (NTSB) investigators said they believed parts of the jet may have broken off before the Sunday crash, which killed all nine on board.

The NTSB found "the majority" of the Learjet's left elevator - a control surface on the tail - in three pieces, NTSB board member Tom Chapman said.

Chapman said because the parts were "quite some distance away" from the site, investigators determined they fell off before impact.

Three years before crash, young co-pilot spoke of earning pilot licence

"Knowing that it was lost is a very important piece of evidence in terms of pointing us towards other things to investigate," he said.

Chapman said officials were still working out whether losing the left elevator caused the plane to nosedive or whether the part fell off because "forces on the aircraft caused that to separate".

He said the part of the plane that controls upward and downward movement may not have been "effective" at the speed the plane was travelling.

The Learjet 60 was bound for the Bahamas from Austin, Texas, when it nosedived into a multi-lane highway and exploded on impact.

In air traffic control audio recordings, someone in the cockpit can be heard reporting "moderate turbulence" and that the crew was "managing the ice".

They are then heard saying the plane was "picking up precipitation" and "we need to climb". Moments later, the same voice said the plane was descending at a rate of 10,000ft (3,048m) per minute.

An emergency was declared and the pilots can be heard yelling "we are in an overspeed condition descending 20,000ft per minute", before saying, "we are going to crash into the ground".

NTSB plans to investigate the role weather played in the crash. Officials were struggling to access the wreckage due to the huge crater created by the jet on a road in Palm Beach.

Chapman said on Tuesday that the force of the impact severely damaged the wreckage.

Seven passengers died on board, named in a joint statement as tech entrepreneur Adam Robinson, his wife, Helen Sharp, and their two children - Emma, four, and Charlie, two - as well as friends of the family Rachael Reed and Charmi Fehr, and Anita Maria Cetina, a nanny.

The pilot was named in media reports as 55-year-old Jeffrey Gregory. Nathalia Filgueiras, 20, who earned her pilot's licence when she was 17, was reported to be the co-pilot.

Netflix just added AI Discovery as 'test kitchen' for discovering content — here's how to find and use it

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Netflix announced today (Nov. 3) that the streaming giant is rolling out a new "AI Discover" tab as an experimental way for you to discover new shows and movies. The first version of the new feature will give you recommendations based on your mood.

Netflix describes the first experience as "built around themes." You can pick from a range of themes like "Scare Me," "Family Movie Night," or "Quick Watches" that come coupled with a clip from a show or movie. Other somewhat more generic ones include what's trending or based on what you've been watching.

Selecting a theme brings up recommendations. Or you can choose "Something Different" for a new set of themes.

There's also a "Tune Your Recommendations" button that you can use to keep a recommendation or remove the selected show or movie from your recommendations.

"Think of AI Discover as our test kitchen: we’re trying out new recipes for discovery, and your use and feedback help us decide which ones to keep, improve, or serve up next. Some may even make their way into the main Netflix experience."

This isn't Netflix's first foray into AI. Earlier this year Netflix released a research model, dubbed VOID, that acts like a super version of "Magic Eraser." It's something you can try right now if you use Hugging Face and you have at least 40GB of VRAM.

In 2025, Netflix played around with generative AI-powered ads that were supposed to launch this year. These ads are meant to be more personalized and less disruptive. However, those ads don't appear to have launched yet.

Sign up to the weekly Tom's Streaming Guide to stay in the loop with the latest releases and top recommendations across your favorite streaming services. We’ve got you covered with a complete guide on what’s worth watching.

Meanwhile, Variety reported in July that more 300 Netflix titles used generative AI this year.

Netflix is rolling out the AI Discover tab to English-language Netflix profiles in the United States on supported TVs. The company says it will open up to more people in the coming weeks.

If you have it, you should see a red gem with "Discover" in the top menu next to My Netflix.

It's not clear how long the rollout will take. I checked on an older Sony Bravia set and a Roku stick on a dumb TV but neither version showed the new tab. Keep an eye out for it.

Birmingham City 3-0 Millwall: Highlights, man of the match, stats as Max Bird solves August Priske problem

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

By Carter White | 03 Nov 2026 21:53, Last updated: 03 Nov 2026 22:40

Birmingham City recorded their third straight home Championship success with a 3-0 victory over Millwall at St Andrew's @ Knighthead Park on Tuesday night.

After netting seven goals in their previous two home matches, Blues were at their attacking best once again from the off in the Second City, as Alex Cochrane fired his side ahead within the opening minute.

Making his full second-tier debut for Birmingham, Max Bird doubled the hosts' advantage at the 16-minute mark before providing the assist for Jay Stansfield's second of the season to put Millwall to rest in the contest.

The Lions were improved in the second period following a half-time formation change, however, the damage had already been inflicted by a Blues side who are allowing their supporters to dream of a long-awaited Premier League return.

Carter White live from St Andrew's @ Knighthead Park

Despite high-profile absentees in goal and up front in the form of James Beadle and August Priske, Birmingham enjoyed another comfortable home victory after smashing Preston North End and Queens Park Rangers in October.

As was the case against bottom side Preston, Blues raced into a three-goal lead before the half-time break, all-but deciding the destination of the three points before the second period had even started.

It is now five wins in the past six for Chris Davies's troops, who are continuing to prove their worth as serious promotion candidates, moving into the top two courtesy of an impressive Second City success.

As for the visitors, Millwall have now lost four of their opening seven away contests of the Championship campaign, equalling the number of defeats that they endured across 23 outings during the 2025-26 term.

Alex Neil's men have painfully misplaced their winning formula on the road and it has led to another chastening loss at the hands of Blues, who smashed four past the Lions in last season's contest at St Andrew's.

What a start for Alex Cochrane and Birmingham! ? https://t.co/xMZ0eliwFB

What an exceptional start for Birmingham!

Blues' first attack of the contest comes from a free kick which is partially cleared by Caleb Taylor to the edge of the box, where a goal-getting left-back was ready to pounce on the loose ball.

After netting at Sincil Bank against Lincoln City on Saturday, Cochrane strode towards the ball and unleashed a low-driven strike, with Millwall goalkeeper Max Crocombe rooted to the spot as the effort evaded him.

"What a beginning to the game!" ? Max Bird doubles Birmingham's lead ? https://t.co/uqtP06vPYp

Birmingham double their advantage, and it's summer arrival Bird with an excellent finish!

Jhon Solis wins a second ball in the middle of the Second City park and directs his header towards the former Bristol City man, who skips ahead of Derek Mazou-Sacko to earn a free run at the Millwall backline.

The 26-year-old worked his way into the visitors' box before shifting the ball to the left, moving a yard away from Taylor and firing a low effort past the outstretched Crocombe in net.

Birmingham are running riot against Millwall! ? https://t.co/qK9SJe5nTb

Birmingham are cruising towards a third home victory of the season!

Stansfield drifts out to the right-hand side and is found courtesy of a pinpoint pass by attacking partner Bird, with the former running at the disorganised Millwall rearguard with serious intent.

Both Taylor and Jake Cooper are quick to recognise the threat of the £20m man, however, Stansfield gets the better of them both, earning a yard to the left and placing a strike past Crocombe at his near post.

Priske's absence this evening resulted in Stansfield operating as the central striker, meaning that Davies had a hole to fill at the tip of midfield, and it is safe to say that the Birmingham boss made the right selection.

Starting his first Championship match for Blues, Bird took advantage of acres of space in the middle of the park, with the hosts overloading a Millwall midfield pairing of Mazou-Sacko and Camiel Neghli in the first half.

The 26-year-old netted his first competitive goal since February to double Birmingham's lead during the opening 20 minutes before turning provider for his side's third, feeding an excellent ball through to goalscorer Stansfield.

Blues have struggled when leading goalscorer Priske has missed out at points during the early stages of this term but that was not the case tonight, with Bird proving that Davies's side can survive without their Danish marksman.

Possession: Birmingham City 55%-45% Millwall

Shots on target: Birmingham City 8-2 Millwall

Now looking to record four consecutive home wins, Birmingham welcome promotion rivals Swansea City to St Andrew's on Saturday afternoon, when the hosts will be looking to earn their eighth league victory of the season.

Millwall are back in the West Midlands on the same day, with the wounded Lions facing West Bromwich Albion at The Hawthorns, where the visiting faithful will demand a much-improved performance.

Burrage receives death threats and abuse after Chennai Open defeat

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jodie Burrage has revealed online abuse and death threats; both Burrage and fellow British player Katie Boulter believe most abuse comes from tennis gamblers who have lost money; Warning: This article contains details some readers may find distressing

Britain's Jodie Burrage has received death threats and abuse on social media after she was beaten in the first round of the Chennai Open on Tuesday.

Burrage, who was playing her first WTA Tour singles match in 16 months, went down against India's Vaishnavi Adkar.

She looked poised for victory when she led 5-3 in the deciding set but lost four games in a row to fall to a 3-6 6-3 7-5 defeat after two hours and 47 minutes.

The 27-year-old, who reached a career-high ranking 85 in September 2023, published an Instagram story with some of the abuse received due to the defeat.

"Nice welcome back to tour. I remember why I left," Burrage said in the accompanying message.

German player Eva Lys tagged the WTA from her Instagram handle and asked: "When will something be done?"

The 27-year-old appeared to struggle with the humid conditions and called the doctor with her opponent about to serve for the match.

Burrage was visibly shaking and had her blood pressure and oxygen levels checked as well as ice rubbed on her arms and legs. She was able to resume the contest but lost the final game to love.

Last July, Burrage urged gambling companies and social media companies to do more to tackle online trolls.

Speaking after her first-round defeat to American Caty McNally at Wimbledon, Burrage said she has had to stop herself from looking at her phone due to online abuse.

"I think it's very simple to have someone's ID connected to their account and that would, I think, quickly stop what people write and make them more accountable," Burrage said.

"I'm just guessing that they're gamblers; some of them say, 'You've lost me this amount of money' or whatever, so some of them are. I don't know, that's the only explanation I've got otherwise they are just very bad people.

"But yes, it's tough and today I'm trying not to look at my phone. It's not easy to deal with, but I do think there could be more being done."

A report from the Women's Tennis Association and International Tennis Federation revealed that 458 players were direct targets of abuse in 2024. The report, produced using an AI-led detection system developed by the Signify Group, found that 40 per cent of the social media abuse came from frustrated gamblers, a figure that rose to 77 per cent for direct abuse directed towards players' personal accounts.

Katie Boulter also opened up about the level of abuse aimed at some players and said she and her family have received death threats last year.

Boulter believes much of the abuse comes from gamblers who have lost money and while she has learned how to better handle it over the years, incidents can raise genuine concerns for her safety.

"I think it just kind of shows how vulnerable we are. You really don't know if this person is on site. You really don't know if they're nearby or if they know where you live or anything like that," she said.

"At the very start of my career, it's probably something I took very personally [...] getting comments about the way you look."

"It becomes more apparent every single time you go on your phone."

The WTA's player board has previously condemned the abuse as "unacceptable" and said the impact can be "significant".

Air Tahiti Nui Will Offer Free Starlink Wi-Fi On Its Boeing 787s As Of 2027

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Air Tahiti Nui has just announced a massive upgrade to its inflight connectivity, as the airline will be rolling out free Starlink Wi-Fi across its (small) fleet in 2027.

Air Tahiti Nui has revealed that it plans to install Starlink Wi-Fi on its entire fleet, which consists of four Boeing 787-9s. The airline plans to offer this service for free to passengers in all travel classes.

Starlink will be introduced in 2027, though no timeline beyond that is known yet, so it could be early or late in the year. Presumably once installation starts, it’ll be completed very quickly — four planes could easily be fitted with Starlink in a single week.

Air Tahiti Nui operates some very long flights — including from Papeete (PPT) to Paris (CDG) via Los Angeles (LAX) so having fast and free connectivity is super valuable. While so many airlines market their Wi-Fi as being high speed, Starlink is the only product currently in service where you can consistently stream videos, etc.

Currently, Air Tahiti Nui’s Dreamliners are equipped with Panasonic Wi-Fi, which actually has relatively decent speeds. The catch is that Air Tahiti Nui chooses to have insanely high pricing based primarily on data usage, rather than just offering a full flight pass for a fixed amount.

Below is Air Tahiti Nui’s current pricing chart for Wi-Fi… could they make this any more complicated? $28 for 100 MB of data is absurd, and $98 for unlimited data is even more ridiculous.

This kind of pricing just strikes me as being so stupid. All you end up doing is frustrating passengers, and I also have to imagine this isn’t actually the system that maximizes usage. It’s weird to have a provider with speeds that are actually semi-usable (Panasonic is better than OnAir), and to then do this with it.

So anyway, Starlink will simply be a massive upgrade for Air Tahiti Nui in every way. Speaking of which, I’d sure love to return to French Polynesia at some point (and fly that all-business class Air Tahiti turboprop, while I’m at it).

In 2027, Air Tahiti Nui intends to install Starlink Wi-Fi on its fleet of four Dreamliners. It remains to be seen exactly when in 2027 it’ll happen, but this represents a huge upgrade to the passenger experience. The airline currently offers Panasonic Wi-Fi with awful data-based pricing (or an unlimited package that will run you close to $100).

What do you make of Air Tahiti Nui introducing Starlink?

Bridget Fonda Makes Rare Appearance 24 Years After Last Acting Role

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Single White Female actress Bridget Fonda made a rare appearance in Los Angeles Nov. 2, over 24 years after she retired from acting following the 2002 mini series Snow Queen.

Bridget Fonda is finding a life away from Hollywood.

The Single White Female actress made a rare public appearance in Los Angeles Nov. 2, over 24 years after she last graced the screen in the 2002 mini series Snow Queen.

The 62-year-old kept it casual for her lunch outing with a friend, wearing a white T-shirt paired with black sweats and an oversized yellow button-down shirt. She completed her look with a black bag, white sneakers and her hair in a ponytail.

Fonda has done her best to stay out of the spotlight since retiring from acting, instead keeping her focus on her 23-year marriage to musician and composer Danny Elfman and raising their 21-year-old son Oliver Elfman.

And as the Jackie Brown actress admitted, she has no desire to return to her acting career, telling a photographer in 2023, per the Daily Mail, “It’s too nice being a civilian.”

Being a civilian has also helped set her apart from the rest of her famous family, as Fonda is the granddaughter of classic Hollywood star Henry Fonda, the daughter of actor Peter Fonda and his first wife Susan Brewer, as well as the niece of actress Jane Fonda.

And despite growing up among Hollywood royalty and raised by a father who was a leading figure in the counterculture movement with his 1969 film Easy Rider, Fonda has been candid about how normal her childhood was.

“People imagine that I was dumped in the corner of a series of drug-fuelled orgies, and that we were like savages in the bush,” she told The Guardian in 2000, “but it wasn't like that at all. I grew up around adults who were imaginative and spontaneous, sure, and it was fantastic to go on that ride.”

Though she had an unconventional childhood—recalling how she would hang out on Malibu beach with her godfather Larry Hagman—Fonda’s parents also emphasized “discipline” with her.

“I called everyone mister and missus, like friends' parents and teachers, even though it was a time when everyone was insisting on being called by their first names,” Fonda said. “I always washed my hands before I ate, and used a napkin.”

“My dad may have been a hippy biker in the public mind,” she added, “but he was always a classy, well-turned-out hippy.”

For a deep dive into more stars who have stepped away from Hollywood, keep reading.

After the Mask of Zorro actor suffered a near-fatal heart attack in 2019, he moved back to his native Málago, Spain, to focus on his love of theater.

“It changed the way I look at life,” Banderas told The Times in March 2026. “Faced with death, it made me look back and realise that I am, in fact, a theater actor.”

Now, the Tony nominee—who still occasionally takes on TV and film projects—owns a theater venue and gets involved with the local community.

“I have never been so happy,” he continued. “It keeps me in touch with my roots, my neighbourhood, my people. I haven’t lost that connection over the years. Quite the opposite.”

Before landing a role in Prime Video's Every Year After, The Girl Next Door actress took a four-year break from acting, leaving L.A. to raise her two kids with husband Dion Phaneuf.

"I realized I worked all four years through our first child and it was really hard," Cuthbert confessed in June 2026 during an appearance on Today, "to separate that mom from the working person I was. So, when we had our second, I just felt like I didn't want to waste any second of it and I didn't want to be on set."

Cuthbert—best known for starring in '00s films like Old School as well as hit TV shows including 24 and Happy Endings—added, "I just felt like I needed to be at home with the kids and I enjoyed every minute."

The actress, known for her roles on hit TV dramas like Fire Country, Numb3rs and Rescue Me, shared in July 2026 that she sold her Los Angeles home after living there for 30 years to embark on her "third chapter" somewhere far away from Hollywood.

"I came here chasing a dream that I am still shocked worked out," she wrote on Instagram. "And I also got to raise babies in the sun and the mountains and enjoy this beautiful home and attend public schools ?? Pinch me I’m overwhelmed by the blessings and hope, really hope I gave enough back. LA will always be where my family is from."

The Ellen DeGeneres Show host's eponymous talk show ended in 2022 after a 19-season run amid allegations of a toxic work environment that prompted an internal investigation, multiple firings of executives and public ire.

DeGeneres returned to the spotlight for her final standup special For Your Approval, which released on Netflix in September 2024. During the lead-up to that special, she revealed she would be leaving Hollywood altogether.

"This is the last time you’re going to see me," she told a crowd during her Ellen’s Last Stand…Up tour, per SFGate. “After my Netflix special, I’m done."

DeGeneres and her wife Portia de Rossi have since moved to the Cotswolds, deciding to stay put in November 2024 after Donald Trump's reelection, the talk show host told to Richard Bacon in a July 2025 conversation at the Everyman theater in Cheltenham.

"It's absolutely beautiful," she gushed about the English countryside, per the BBC. "We're just not used to seeing this kind of beauty. The villages and the towns and the architecture—everything you see is charming and it's just a simpler way of life.

"It's clean," DeGeneres continued. "Everything here is just better—the way animals are treated, people are polite. I just love it here. We moved here in November, which was not the ideal time, but I saw snow for the first time in my life. We love it here. Portia flew her horses here, and I have chickens, and we had sheep for about two weeks."

One of the reasons the America's Next Top Model host moved to Australia? To stay on top of her ice cream brand."So I have this ice cream company, SMiZE and Dream," Banks explained on an April 2025 episode of Today with Jenna & Friends. “But we've been more what we call B2B, like on an airplane and on cruises. I’ve been going to Australia a lot because we were making a lot of our ice cream there in a big facility doing our recipes. And I just fell in love with it every time I went back and went back."Not only was she happy there, but she also knew it was a good market for her business."The three countries that eat the most ice cream?" she continued. "America, New Zealand and Australia."So, the supermodel moved to Australia with her son York (his dad is her ex Erik Asla) and boyfriend Louis Bélanger-Martin, noting, "Australia has been a dream come true for me and my family."

While it's been a minute since the Desperate Housewives star lived on Wisteria Lane, she's since found a new address IRL."I had my whole adult life here," Eva told Marie Claire in November 2024 about leaving Los Angeles. "But even before [the pandemic], it was changing. The vibe was different. And then Covid happened, and it pushed it over the edge. Whether it’s the homelessness or the taxes, not that I want to s--t on California—it just feels like this chapter in my life is done now."So for the past few years, the actress, her husband José Bastón and their son Santiago have split their time between Mexico and Spain."I’m privileged," she continued. "I get to escape and go somewhere. Most Americans aren’t so lucky. They’re going to be stuck in this dystopian country, and my anxiety and sadness is for them."However, Eva noted her move wasn't because of President Trump's re-election."I didn't leave because of the political environment," the Flamin' Hot director said on The View: Behind the Table podcast in November 2024. "I left because my work took me there since Land of Women—shooting six months in Catalonia, then four months in Mexico for Searching for Mexico, then back to Spain. Now I've been there for years. So I just don't like that it's politicized."Since moving, Eva has continued to stay busy, with recent projects including her roles in Only Murders in the Building and Alexander and the Terrible, Horrible, No Good, Very Bad Road Trip.

The actor has gone from Chicago to Spain."My wife is Spanish, and she gave me about seven years here," Richard said of Galicia native Alejandra Silva on a November 2024 episode of The Tonight Show Starring Jimmy Fallon. "And so we're going to spend some years in Madrid."He also expressed his excitement over what the move would give the couple's young sons Alexander and James."Our kids are bilingual," Richard, who also has an adult son Homer with ex Carey Lowell, added, "so they're going to flourish there."Indeed, it seems the whole family has."The truth is that you are seeing us in our momentum," The Agency: Central Intelligence star told Elle España in January 2025. "We are happier than ever."

From AM to PM, the singer is living in Paris "My husband is French, and we've been together for over seven or eight years," Milian, who is married to singer Matt Pokora, said on a November 2024 episode of The Jennifer Hudson Show. "And for the last three or four years we were commuting back and forth every six months."However, they decided to officially say au revoir to the U.S. so her kids—who include daughter Violet from her former marriage with The-Dream and sons Isaiah and Kenna from her union with Pokora—could study in France."They're all going to school in Paris," she continued. "They know French. We go to the park. I have an active lifestyle that's really nice during the day. Then at night time, I do my work because I work hours ahead. It's a nice, healthy balance for me."

The Mean Girls star is tired of rumors starting about why she left Los Angeles.So, she set the record straight while looking back at her hectic work schedule and partying in the aughts. "I feel like some of [my work] got overshadowed by paparazzi and all that kind of stuff when I was younger, and that’s kind of annoying," the Parent Trap alum told Bustle in March 2024. I was like, 'Unless there’s no story here, they’re not going to focus on just my work.'"Lohan moved abroad—first to London then to Dubai (also opening beach clubs in Greece). However, there was another reason she sought a life away from the spotlight.

"I was like, 'What if I never fall in love? What if this never happens?'" she added. The Internals of PostgreSQL

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After a temporary stay in Japan to focus on my health and personal responsibilities, I am fully recovered, ready, and excited to work abroad again.

I am highly interested in returning to Europe, as well as opportunities in the Southern Hemisphere and Southeast Asia.

If your team is hiring, letâs connect! Please feel free to reach out via e-Mail(hironobu @ interdb.jp or interdb.mx @ gmail.com) or DM.

PostgreSQL is a well-designed, open-source multi-purpose relational database system which is widely used throughout the world.

It is one huge system with the integrated subsystems, each of which has a particular complex feature and works cooperatively with each other. Although understanding of the internal mechanism is crucial for both administration and integration using PostgreSQL, its hugeness and complexity make it difficult.

The main purposes of this documentation are to explain how each subsystem works, and to provide the whole picture of PostgreSQL.

This documentation covers versions 19-beta (excluding the new buffer page lock mechanism) and earlier.

Some academic papers have referred to this documentation. The Chinese version of this documentation was published in June 2019.

This documentation was first published on 26 September 2015.

I am a software programmer/engineer, the author of:

I graduated from graduate school in information engineering (M.S. in Information Engineering), have worked for several companies as a software developer and technical manager/director. I published seven books in the fields of database and system integration (4 PostgreSQL books and 3 MySQL books). In June 2019, the Chinese book of this documentation was published.

As a director of the Japan PostgreSQL Users Group (2010-2016), I organized the largest (non-commercial) technical seminar/lecture on PostgreSQL in Japan for more than six years, and also served as the program committee chair of the Japan PostgreSQL Conference in 2013 and as a member in 2008 and 2009.

In June 2022, my interview article was published in “PostgreSQL person of the week”.

I am seeking a remote position focused on the application of ML and AI technologies to DBMS. For personal reasons, I will be living in Japan for several years.

Since publishing my content, I’ve been fortunate to receive a lot of positive feedback, which is truly gratifying. However, unfortunately, I’ve encountered individuals in the past who tried to misuse my content for self-promotion 1.

Chris Rock Details How Porn Affected His Nearly 20-Year Marriage

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Chris Rock elaborated on issues—including a porn addiction and cheating—that led to his divorce from Malaak Compton-Rock, his wife of almost 20 years, in 2016.

Chris Rock is thinking about how he could've loved his wife better.

The Madagascar star discussed how a porn addiction and infidelity impacted his marriage to Malaak Compton-Rock, whom he married in 1996 before their 2016 divorce.

"I was not a great husband. I watched a lot of porn," Chris told GQ in an interview published Nov. 3. "I don’t watch porn anymore. I haven’t watched porn in five, six years."

The 61-year-old previously spoke on how the issue impacted his split from Malaak—with whom he shares daughters, Lola, 24, and Zahra, 22—in 2018's Tambourine. During the comedy special, he also confessed to cheating on the entrepreneur, which he looks back on today as a difficult learning lesson.

As he put it, "It’s not anything I’m proud of, but it’s something I had to go through."

In fact, Chris compared opening up about his marital woes on Netflix to Johnny Cash singing the blues.

Still, the Everybody Hates Chris comedian embraces the difficult time as a part of his story, likening the unfaithfulness to a superhero's fall from grace in an action movie.

"It’s like you watch a Marvel movie, and the guy, he has to test his powers," Chris elaborated. "I tested my powers. I definitely realized I wasn’t God or anywhere near him, and I was severely humbled, as I should have been."

As such, Chris turned to therapy to help with multiple areas of his life—and the process was admittedly emotional.

"Put it this way: My personal life wasn’t what I wanted it to be," he continued. "And that’s my relationship with everybody. So yeah, I went in there crying like a b---h. Like, 'What the f--k is going on?'"

Preston North End 2 Sheffield United 3: Hat-trick hero Sydie Peck steals show for Blades

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The silky midfielder’s previous goal this season had arrived not far away from Preston at Ewood Park.

He must have a liking for these parts after plundering a first-half hat-trick in less than 20 minutes, becoming the first Blades player to achieve that feat since the legendary Billy Sharp in February 2019.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

A thoroughly bizarre first period saw United stink the place out in the opening 20 minutes and deservedly trail rock-bottom hosts, who led after just two minutes, courtesy of Leo Leroy’s first goal for North End.

Fortunately, United eventually got with it, thanks to the elegant Peck.

A 76th-minute goal from Preston substitute Callum Lang would set up an edgy finale at 2-3. It has rarely been easy and straightforward where United have been concerned over the past couple of seasons.

But that night was theirs and Peck’s as Wilder’s side recorded back-to-back league wins for the first time since April.

It’s the time of year when the nights draw in, the overcoats come out and league tables start to mean that bit more.

Five games into a seven-match block of Championship games ahead of the November international break, Wilder’s side were in reasonably good shape - certainly in comparison to this time last season - with 10 points accrued from a possible 15.

Being consistently inconsistent remains something that the Blades had been wrestling with and against a Preston side on the ropes all season and propping up the rest virtually since day one, it was an evening for getting the job done.

North End definitely weren’t in good shape. The arrival of Graham Alexander from Bradford City had failed to arrest their fortunes, quite the opposite in fact. They had taken a derisory one point from his opening five games.

Preston came into the game with a horrific statistic against their name, with the only side in Championship history to muster few points after the first 13 games of a campaign - without points deductions - being Sheffield Wednesday back in 2023-24.

That campaign, the Owls won in game 14 and managed to stay up. The odds of Preston doing that appear long, indeed.

Kalvin Phillips, Joe Rothwell and Patrick Bamford all missed out through injury, with Jairo Riedewald handed his first league start since September 5. Mark McGuinness came in for the suspended Liam Kitching, while ex-North End loanee Tom Cannon was back up front for United.

The game started off in the worst possible fashion for United, wearing their luminous yellow away strip.

Tahith Chong was painfully weak in the challenge and home captain Andrew Hughes powered forward and found Andrija Vuckevic down the left in acres of space on the overlap.

Andrew Moran failed to connect with a fresh-air shot following Vuckevic’s low centre, but Leroy - left untracked after powering into the box - was unerring, blasting home a first-timer into the top corner.

It was a dreadful start for United, who were safe, passive and meek in the opening 20 minutes. It certainly did not look like a Wilder team with the hosts outworking and outenthusing those in visiting colours and winning virtually all of the duels.

The midfielder delivered a precious nugget of much-needed quality on 27 minutes from the left, where United were far more convincing than on the opposite flank.

Peck’s sweetly struck cross-shot evaded everyone before nestling in the far corner.

It was the prelude to Preston starting to display the brittle defensive confidence that you would associate with a side marooned at the foot of the table.

Daniel Iversen’s decision to race out of goal was ill-advised in the extreme following Ben Brereton Diaz’s astute pass and he found himself in total no-man’s land.

Still, Peck had so much to do after rounding the home keeper on the left-hand side.

With North End defenders on the cover and team-mates in support, Peck had a decision to make. Play it safe and pass or go for it?

He chose the latter and his marvellous curled shot was perfection and flew into the top corner. It was a thing of beauty not in keeping with United's first 20 minutes.

Their fans had been getting understandably edgy against seemingly pumped-up hosts. The half would end with considerable home tension and even chants of ‘You’re not fit to wear the shirt’ ahead of boos at the interval.

Peck’s hat-trick goal was the reason with Romelle Donovan’s low centre dissecting a dozing North End defence, with Peck obligingly tapping home from close in.

Early in the second half, Peck - clearly getting a taste for it and enjoying himself immensely - almost added another, testing Iversen low down at his near post.

The Peck show continued. A free-kick was blocked and an angled shot almost crept in at the far corner

The next goal was a Preston one as North End secured a lifeline after Lang coolly converted following an assist from fellow replacement Alfie Devine with the visitors opened up in central defence.

It made it a needlessly tense finale. But the night was Peck’s.

Preston North End: Iversen; Offiah, Storey (Lindsay HT), Hughes; Mills (Potts 90), Leroy, Thompson, Vukcevic (Wiley HT) Moran; Erabi (Devine 64), Kenny (Lang 13). Substitutes unused: Nicholls, Small, Nolan, Burgzorg.

Sheffield United: Cooper; Choudhury (Ayew 71), Tanganga, McGuinness, Burrows; Chong (Seriki 58), Peck, Riedewald (Arblaster 58), Donovan (McCallum 84); Cannon, Brereton-Diaz. Substitutes unused: Doherty, Campbell, Norrington-Davies, Betts, Baptiste.

AMD stock jumps 8% on earnings as data center revenue nearly doubles

📅 2026-11-03  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Advanced Micro Devices reported third-quarter earnings on Tuesday that beat Wall Street's expectations, with revenue jump 45% and data center sales nearly doubling from a year earlier. The stock jumped 8% in extended trading.

Here's how the chipmaker did versus LSEG consensus estimates for the quarter:

Revenue increased from $9.25 billion a year earlier, AMD said in a statement.

CEO Lisa Su said in a press release that she expects growth in data center processors to "accelerate further," and that meeting demand won't be a problem.

"With our leadership product portfolio and deep customer and supply chain relationships, we are confident in our ability to sustain very strong revenue growth and expanding profitability over the coming years " Su said in a statement.

AMD's report comes days after Intel disclosed similar data center growth, with revenue in that segment up 78% in the third quarter. But the stock plummeted 29% on Friday over concerns about weak guidance and Intel's ability to meet demand.

The data center unit makes up the majority of AMD's business, accounting for 64% of overall revenue, up from 47% a year ago.

After years spent chasing Nvidia in the market for artificial intelligence graphics processing units, or GPUs, AMD's central processing units, or CPUs, are experiencing a major resurgence with the rise of agentic AI apps like Meta's Muse. AMD's server CPUs are key for agentic apps because they power virtual machines and can run for hours on their own. Demand from agentic AI boosted AMD shares to a record in October.

"Adoption is just starting, and we expect demand to grow sharply as agents are deployed more broadly and take on more complex work," Su said on the earnings call with analysts.

AMD's stock is up about 125% this year, but it saw three consecutive days of losses before rallying on Tuesday amid an industrywide sell-off tied to fears about overspending on AI.

AMD is also a major player the in weakening PC market, which Intel blamed for its poor outlook last week. AMD and Intel are the largest makers of traditional x86 processors that power many laptops and desktops, while other laptops rely on Arm-based processors from the likes of Qualcomm and Apple.

Global PC shipments declined 4% in the second quarter from a year earlier due to soaring costs for memory and other components, according to Counterpoint Research.

AMD reports PC chip sales in a segment combined with gaming chips. Revenue in that business fell 8% from a year earlier to $3.7 billion. Gaming revenue was down 40% from a year ago.

For the fourth quarter, AMD said it expects about $17 billion in revenue, beating the $16.2 billion average analyst estimate, according to LSEG. AMD sees its gross margin for the quarter coming in at 55%, slightly below the average estimate of 55.3%, according to StreetAccount.

AMD also sells data center GPUs and full rack-scale systems called Helios that compete directly with Nvidia's Grace Blackwell and Vera Rubin. Meta, Microsoft, Anthropic, OpenAI, and Oracle are all beginning to use AMD Helios in their data centers. Meta said last week that its Helios racks are up and running, and AMD agreed to invest $1 billion in Meta's equity offering.

AMD has also committed to investing up to $5 billion in Anthropic, which unveiled its highly anticipated prospectus last week as it inches closer to a potentially historic initial public offering.

During a visit to Taiwan in October, Su said she plans to "substantially increase our supply in 2027." Su and Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang have both made multiple trips to Taiwan and South Korea this year, holding meetings with key suppliers like Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company and memory leaders SK Hynix and Samsung.

In September, AMD said it would acquire World Labs for $8.2 billion in an all-stock deal. The San Francisco-based AI lab, founded by industry pioneer Fei-Fei Li, is developing a so-called world model that simulates 3D environments to aid in developing robots and other physically grounded AI applications.

SOON traders, look beyond positive funding before betting on a recovery

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Soon [SOON] started the day on a bearish note, with the asset declining 14%, according to CoinGlass. However, market activity shows that the current price level has not changed the fundamental factors hinting at a possible bullish base still being in place.

The classic explanation for this is that while SOON’s short-term outlook remains bearish, its long-term prospects still show strong upside potential.

The broader market shock has played a key role in pulling capital out of the asset. Press-time data shows that SOON has recorded significant outflows in both the perpetual and spot markets.

In the perpetual market, the netflow data shows heavy selling activity. CoinGlass shows that capital outflows reached roughly $10.44 million, compared to inflows of $8.62 million, putting its net inflow at -$1.82 million.

A negative net inflow in the perpetual market often suggests that traders are pulling capital out of the market, reflecting heightened volatility.

In the spot market, the outlook is similar, with spot net inflows turning positive. In this case, a positive net inflow suggests that more SOON is being moved into exchanges than out of them, potentially indicating that traders are preparing to sell rather than accumulate.

Press-time data shows a spot net inflow of $697,000, as more capital moved into exchanges while traders prepared to sell off the asset, possibly in reaction to bearish market conditions.

The current capital movements across the perpetual and spot markets appear to reflect short-term market activity rather than a long-term shift.

The liquidation heatmap, on the other hand, points to a potentially bullish outlook for SOON over the near to long term. CoinGlass’s 1-month liquidation heatmap shows that liquidation clusters are heavily concentrated above the current price, with barely any visible clusters below it.

Price tends to move towards liquidation clusters on the chart, and right now, their heavy concentration above the current level could pull the asset towards those levels.

Importantly, the limited clusters below the price indicate that sellers could run into exhaustion. If demand grows, SOON could see a rebound, potentially creating new local highs.

For now, momentum is against long traders in the market, with $67,770 in losses recorded by long traders compared to just $4,440 in losses for short traders over the past 24 hours.

Traders in the perpetual market remain bullish across SOON contracts, based on funding rate data.

The funding rate measures whether long or short positions dominate an asset’s perpetual contracts. Presently, long positions dominate the market, as the funding rate remains positive at approximately 0.0051%, according to CoinGlass.

Bullish positioning at a time when prices are weak and the market is declining suggests that a potential rebound could still play out in the near term. Overall, capital in the perpetual market, as reflected in the funding rate data, still sits at $32.32 million at the time of writing.

JetBlue raises bag fees for third time in a year as fuel costs bite

📅 2026-11-03  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

JetBlue Airways has raised the price of checking a bag for many customers for the third time in less than a year as high fuel costs continue to eat into the industry's profits.

Checking a first bag for travelers booked in economy will now be $50, up from $45, if purchased more than 24 hours before departure during a nonpeak travel period. If it's paid within 24 hours of departure, the fee will be $60. During a peak period, such as during the holidays or the summer, it will be $55 if purchased more than 24 hours before departure, up from $49, and $70 for within 24 hours of departure.

There are exceptions to the checked bag fees, such as for customers who hold a co-branded JetBlue credit card or who hold elite frequent flyer status.

"We're adjusting checked bag fees to help offset rising fuel and operating costs," JetBlue said in a statement. "This helps us keep our base fares competitive while giving customers the choice to pay for a checked bag only if they need one."

The price of jet fuel, airlines' biggest cost after labor, has roughly doubled this year since the start of the Iran war in February, costing airlines billions and driving up the price of tickets. Jet fuel was going for $4.44 a gallon on Tuesday for the U.S. Gulf of Mexico, according to the U.S. Energy Information Administration.

Other airlines often follow when one carrier raises fees. Major U.S. airlines did so earlier this year after JetBlue raised its bag fees.

The changes were reported earlier by the Thrifty Traveler blog.

Hill-Climbing Agent Quality with Agents CLI Evaluation

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A live walkthrough of the Agents CLI eval loop, from a first run to regression tracking, model-backend experiments, and automated alerting

Building an agent that works on a demo is a different problem from building an agent that stays working across model upgrades, tool changes, and new conversation shapes. The first takes a weekend; the second takes a loop. A team writes test cases, runs them, grades them, reads what failed, changes the agent, and runs the cases again. If the score goes up, the change was good. If it goes down, revert. Over weeks of this, the agent inches toward the ceiling of what the model, tools, and prompt can do together. The quality-engineering name for this is hill-climbing.

The Agents CLI eval namespace was built around this loop. The commands are small and framework-agnostic: eval generate runs the agent against a dataset of cases, eval grade scores the generated traces, eval compare diffs two run results side by side, eval analyze clusters failures into themes, and eval optimize runs an automated hill-climbing pass on the agent's prompt.

Below: baseline → regression → model swap → scheduling and alerting.

The scaffold used throughout is the LangChain template that ships with Agents CLI v1.8.0. The setup is a single command:

The scaffold ships with an eval dataset at tests/eval/datasets/basic-dataset.json, a config at tests/eval/eval_config.yaml, and a custom LLM-as-judge metric at tests/eval/response_quality.py. The config declares the metric to run:

The metric grades each response on a 1-to-5 scale for accuracy, relevance, and clarity, using gemini-3.8-flash as the judge. The default dataset has a single trivial case, which is not enough for a hill-climb. The first thing we do is expand it to four cases that probe different behaviors:

Four cases: a greeting, a weather question the mock tool handles with the hardcoded foggy response, a weather question for a city the tool defaults on, and an off-topic question that the agent has to decide how to handle. This is a small dataset by design; the hill-climbing loop pays off when the dataset is small enough to run on every change.

With the dataset in place, we run eval generate to drive the agent against each case, then eval grade to score the resulting traces:

Mean 4.0 out of 5 across four cases, with a standard deviation of 1.41. That stdev tells us immediately that the cases are not scoring uniformly, which is a healthier starting point than a flat 5.0 across the board. A flat 5.0 either means the agent is perfect (unlikely) or the dataset is too easy to show variation. 4.0 with real spread is a hill to climb.

To demonstrate the loop, we introduce a realistic regression. A developer working across several agent projects accidentally pastes the wrong system prompt into our weather agent: a chef persona meant for a different codebase. In app/agent.py:

The change compiles, the agent still runs, and nothing in the pre-eval pipeline (lint, unit tests, type checks) catches it. Re-running the same eval generate plus eval grade sequence produces:

Mean 2.75, down from 4.0. The judge agrees with our intuition that a chef persona on a weather agent is a worse product than a general-purpose helpful assistant. The stdev also tightened from 1.41 to 0.96, consistent with the chef prompt pushing most cases toward the same mediocre range.

A 1.25-point drop on a 1-to-5 scale is substantial, and the kind of regression that can slip through code review when the diff looks small ("one string literal changed"). The loop surfaced the change at all, with a specific number attached to it, before the agent reached production.

Mean scores are a start; the per-case diff is where the hill-climb happens. eval compare reads two result files and produces a structured JSON diff:

The output lists which keys changed and which stayed the same:

The differences object has the full per-case breakdown with the baseline and candidate values side by side. For a hill-climbing workflow, we typically pipe the output through jq or a small Python script to extract the signal; the raw JSON is the complete evidence.

Walking the two result files and lining up the scores per case shows where the regression landed:

Three cases dropped, one held. The most important of the three is sf_weather: the chef persona pulled the model's attention away from the registered get_weather tool, so the agent stopped calling it and started making up weather values. Unit tests can't catch this: the code runs, the tool is still registered, but the model's behavior around it has shifted. Only an end-to-end eval that scores the response in context surfaces this.

The mean drop by itself (−1.25) is a clear signal, but the per-case breakdown is what tells the developer where to look. A one-line system-prompt change propagated through three different cases in three different ways: greeting lost a point on tone, sf_weather lost two on tool usage, off_topic lost two on scope creep.

A common hill-climbing question is whether a cheaper or faster model would do the job acceptably. For the LangChain template, the model is defined in app/agent.py:

We swap gemini-3.8-flash for gemini-2.5-flash (restoring the helpful system prompt first), re-run eval generate plus eval grade, and compare the two result files. The mean drops from 4.00 to 2.75, the same magnitude as the chef-prompt regression, but the per-case breakdown tells a completely different story:

The cheaper model matched 3.8-flash exactly on the greeting and rome_weather cases and lost a point on sf_weather (slightly less polished phrasing). The whole visible drop traces to off_topic, where the cheaper model refused with a hedge about the event "not having happened yet" (a training-cutoff artifact), and the judge marked it harshly.

The lesson is that the summary number is misleading. Look only at the mean and you'd call gemini-2.5-flash as bad as the chef prompt. Look at the per-case breakdown and you'd call it a reasonable swap for most of the agent's workload, with one case (handling out-of-scope questions about recent events) exposing a real limitation. If the production distribution of user questions matches the first three cases more than the fourth, the swap is worth considering. If off_topic-style questions are common, it is not.

Two caveats on this experiment. First, model swaps interact with the prompt: a prompt tuned for a stronger model often leaks capability when ported to a weaker one, so the right comparison is "weaker model with a prompt tuned for it" versus "stronger model with its own tuned prompt." Second, the Vertex AI Eval Service calls a judge model to grade the traces, and the judge is held constant across the comparison. Changing the judge is a different experiment and should be done separately.

Agents CLI does not ship a scheduler, but the eval commands are designed to drop into standard infrastructure. The pattern we can use here is a cron job or Cloud Scheduler trigger that runs eval generate plus eval grade on a cadence and writes the results to a persistent location:

A nightly run against a stable regression dataset catches drift from model updates, data changes in the agent's tools, and dependencies that shift under the agent's feet. For a production agent, the scheduled run also captures the model vendor's quiet upgrades, which can move the agent's behavior without any change on our side. The dataset for the scheduled run is typically larger than the one used in the inner loop (dozens to hundreds of cases rather than a few), since it only runs once a day.

The scheduled run produces a result file every day, but a file nobody looks at is not an alert. The pattern here can be a wrapper script that compares each new run's mean against a baseline (the previous run, or a committed known-good) and exits non-zero when the drop exceeds a threshold. The non-zero exit is what triggers the alert from the surrounding scheduler.

Running it against our regression demo (baseline 4.0, candidate 2.75 from the chef-prompt run):

And against an identical pair (self-compare):

The non-zero exit code is what a cron job, Cloud Scheduler, or GitHub Actions step picks up to send the alert.

For ADK projects, eval optimize runs an automated hill-climbing pass on the agent's prompt using the GEPA framework (not currently supported for the LangChain extension). The command takes a dataset and a target metric, proposes prompt variants, grades each one, keeps the variants that improve the metric, and iterates. The output is a set of candidate prompts ranked by their eval score, which a human then reviews and promotes to the agent definition.

Two things to understand about eval optimize. First, it is scoped to prompt optimization, not to arbitrary agent changes: the agent's tools, structure, and model stay fixed. Second, GEPA sometimes finds genuine improvements and sometimes finds prompt variants that score higher on the specific metric by overfitting to its wording. The reviewer is the control. For projects where the loop applies, the discipline is to run eval optimize on the regression dataset (where improvements are measurable) and to review the candidate prompts carefully before promotion.

A few patterns and anti-patterns from running this loop ourselves and watching teams do the same.

Lead with a small, representative dataset, not a comprehensive one. The temptation when starting an eval is to try to cover every edge case the agent will ever see. The result is a dataset that takes an hour to run and that nobody runs on a change. Four cases that run in under a minute, as above, exercise the main behaviors and get run on every change. A regression dataset of three hundred cases that runs for an hour becomes a release gate at best and shelfware at worst.

Snapshot result files, not scores. The score is a summary; the result file is the evidence. When a regression surfaces three weeks after a prompt change, having the full result JSON from before and after the change (not just the mean score) is what lets eval compare show the specific case that moved. The per-case breakdown in Step 4 above would not have been possible from scores alone.

Separate the dataset from its expectations. The dataset is the inputs. The eval config is the expectations. They evolve on different cadences: the dataset grows as new real-world cases surface in production, and the config evolves as the team's definition of "good" matures. Keeping them in separate files, both committed to version control, makes it clear which half changed when a score moves.

Treat summary metrics with suspicion. The model-swap experiment in Step 5 is the clearest example. The mean dropped by half a point, but the per-case breakdown showed the drop was concentrated in one case while the others held or improved. A team that only looked at the mean would have made the wrong call.

More evals do not automatically lead to more fixes. A recent controlled study we ran found that coding agents using agents-cli run four times as many evals as coding agents without it, but change the agent after an eval about as often as the baseline. The behavior gain comes from what the skills put into the build at the start, not from the inner iteration. Evals are necessary for quality, but the quality comes from acting on what the evals show.

Scaffold an Agents CLI project and run the first eval loop in a handful of commands:

The full eval namespace is documented at google.github.io/agents-cli/cli. The source code, including the first-party eval primitives, is at github.com/google/agents-cli.

Cost per task: Using cheap classifiers to route across Gemini models and thinking levels

Fast, reliable A2UI, with swappable inference formats

Smaller apps and faster GPU inference with LiteRT in Google Play services

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

LiteRT in Google Play services is Android's on-device inference runtime for custom models, handling more than a trillion inferences a day across billions of devices. Today, it gets its biggest upgrade yet: GPU inference that runs about 1.4x faster than the legacy GPU delegate, powered by ML Drift and available through the new CompiledModel API, LiteRT’s modern successor to the legacy Interpreter API.

Until now, taking advantage of this faster GPU stack required bundling LiteRT in your app. In our testing, apps that switched to LiteRT in Google Play services are about 8 MB smaller, making them faster to download and lighter on users' devices, because the runtime, including the GPU accelerator, ships once with Play services rather than inside every app. More than 100K Android apps bundle their own copy of LiteRT or TensorFlow Lite today, so the savings add up quickly as apps switch.

If your Android app runs custom ML models on CPU or GPU, we encourage you to switch to LiteRT in Google Play services. You can see it in action in the Google AI Edge Gallery app, where the Scrapbook feature's image segmentation runs on LiteRT in Google Play services.

Developers that adopt LiteRT in Google Play Services enjoy immediate benefits:

LiteRT is the evolution of TensorFlow Lite. It keeps the same .tflite model format you rely on and adds the CompiledModel API, which makes acceleration across fragmented hardware reliable:

Whether you're starting fresh or switching from bundled LiteRT, using LiteRT in Google Play services takes a few lines of code:

If you're already using the legacy Interpreter API in Google Play services, your Interpreter code keeps working on CPU when you switch to play-services-litert. To run on the GPU, move to the CompiledModel API.

Let your coding agent do the work. In Agent Mode, the new LiteRT skill for Android Studio automates the move to LiteRT in Google Play services and the CompiledModel API. Ask it to “migrate my TFLite code to CompiledModel,” or type @litert-runtime, and it switches your app to play-services-litert, rewrites your Interpreter calls, and sets up GPU acceleration with CPU fallback. It can also scaffold a new LiteRT app. Treat it as a strong first pass: review the diff, build, and compare outputs on a real device. Get the skill on GitHub.

Upgrading from legacy APIs? The legacy GPU delegate and custom delegate APIs are deprecated in favor of CompiledModel GPU acceleration and the Compiler Plugin and Dispatch APIs, and NNAPI will be removed starting in 2027. Older SDKs now receive critical security fixes only. Apps you've already shipped keep working, but plan your move to the CompiledModel API now: code that uses deprecated APIs won't compile against the latest SDK, and staying on an older SDK can cause conflicts as your other dependencies move forward. See the release notes for details.

Looking ahead, LiteRT in Google Play services is our foundation for running your own task-specific models on Android, with CPU and GPU acceleration.

Build Generative UI Apps with A2UI & CopilotKit Agents

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Agent-User Interactions are now evolving from text exchanges to rich UIs. Until recently, most conversations looked like this:

User: "Find me a holiday package to Bali in March." Agent: (six packages in prose, each with dates, hotel, flights, price) User: "The five-night one with breakfast." Agent: "Here's the link. You can book it on the operator's site."

Agents could plan the trip, but everything came back as walls of text. They had no standard way to render a selectable list of packages, a map of the destinations, or a date picker to carry you through the rest of the booking, inside the conversation itself.

Generative UI is the layer that lets agents build interfaces at runtime, shaped by what each request needs - giving users a rich, interactive experience. Implementing that takes two pieces: a way to describe the UI and an agentic stack that connects your agent to your application and renders that UI.

Generative UI covers a spectrum of approaches, from using predefined higher order components, to enabling your AI agent to have the flexibility to decide how to build the UI.

At the controlled end, you build each component ahead of time, and the agent picks which one to render and with what data. It's brand-safe but it does not allow for deep customization and personalization based on the user’s intent. At the open end, the agent returns a raw UI program written in HTML, Javascript and/or Dart that renders inside a sandboxed iframe. That covers anything you did not plan for, at the cost of a surface that drifts from your design system and executes code a model wrote.

A2UI works through the entire spectrum. It participates in controlled generation with decision models like Typesafe’s Jev and also open-ended code generation that may output A2UI JSON messages. The declarative mode, where agents assemble pre-declared components and functions, is the most popular. A2UI is the framework-agnostic standard for declaring UI intent, so local or remote agents can generate your UI from your existing component catalog using a common language.

Frontend developers can now ship the building blocks once, and the agent composes them for each request.

Sorry, your browser doesn't support playback for this video

The Basic Catalog ships 18 general-purpose primitives across layout, display, input, and containers. The agent can compose entirely different interfaces from the same 18 primitives, depending on what the request needs.

The Basic Catalog is deliberately sparse, so most production apps define their own custom catalog. A custom component can be anything your frontend already renders, including a live map the agent fills with coordinates while your renderer draws the route.

CopilotKit is built on AG-UI (Agent-User Interaction Protocol), which supports A2UI payload and provides Agent SDKs across many agentic backends.

Your agent emits A2UI operations. AG-UI carries them to the client as typed events. When the user takes any action, the values are sent back along the same stream.

A2UI works with any AG-UI compatible agent. Frameworks like Google ADK, LangChain, Mastra and Strands are all AG-UI compatible, so the setup is identical whichever one your agent runs on.

Setup comes down to two things: connecting your agent to CopilotKit, and defining which components the agent is allowed to use.

Using Google ADK as the example, the ag_ui_adk package wraps any ADK agent and serves it as an AG-UI endpoint on FastAPI:

On the app side, you register that endpoint with the CopilotKit runtime at /api/copilotkit. It's a few lines of standard setup, covered in the Google ADK quickstart, and it needs nothing A2UI-specific.

A2UI itself turns on from the client, where your components already are. Define what the agent is allowed to draw, map each definition to a renderer, and pass the catalog to the CopilotKit provider.

The description goes into the agent's prompt, so the model knows when to reach for FlightCard instead of a generic card. The Zod schema validates the props it sends back.

The A2UI configuration can be set up ahead of time on the server, or it can be created dynamically, shipped from the client to the server with the request. The ADK code above used dynamic configuration which is why it used generate_a2ui without defining it. The docs cover both approaches.

That’s the CopilotKit path. You can also consume the event stream directly with @ag-ui/client and hand the A2UI operations to any compliant renderer: @a2ui/react, or the Flutter, Lit, or Angular equivalent.

Sorry, your browser doesn't support playback for this video

Most agent-generated UI is one-shot. The agent draws something, you read it, and it goes stale the moment anything changes.

A2UI surfaces are bi-directional and the components are reactive, which makes multi-step flows possible. Any action the user takes on the interface (picking dates, choosing a flight, entering passenger details) travels back along the same stream, and the agent re-emits the surface from there using those values.

Here is a high-level architecture example of an end-to-end booking flow, with an AG-UI agent on Cloud Run streaming A2UI surfaces to a CopilotKit frontend.

Two things make the round trip work: inputs bound to a data path, and a button carrying an action that names which paths to send back.

Validation runs on the client, so a bad email shows an error inline without a model call. Nothing reaches the agent until the button fires.

Then the client sends the bound values, and the agent re-emits against the same surfaceId. That is how the whole flow lives on one surface: flight search, results, passenger details, and confirmation.

Sorry, your browser doesn't support playback for this video

Every AG-UI integration inherets these benefits, so you don't have to build them yourself:

Create a complete A2UI + CopilotKit application. This creates a starter A2UI application with Copilotkit Agent integration.

The CopilotKit Dojo has runnable A2UI demos: fixed server-side schema, dynamic schema against a custom catalog, and an error-recovery example where an invalid first render heals into a valid one.

Explore how to build Generative UI Agents with CopilotKit, or dive into A2UI documentation to learn how to integrate A2UI with any framework of your choice.
