Alix Earle Is "Taking Away" More Boundaries About Posting Her Life

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Between Alix Earle sharing her life online and on her reality show, Earle Meets World, the influencer told E! News at the CFDA Awards that she plans to bring fans even further into her inner world.

World, get ready to meet even more of Alix Earle.

As the influencer continues documenting her lavish life on social media and her new reality show Earle Meets World, she revealed she's not planning to pull on the reins anytime soon.

"I'm not so good at drawing boundaries, and I'm not sure that I ever will," Earle exclusively told E! News at the 2026 CFDA Fashion Awards Presented by Amazon Fashion on Nov. 2. "If anything, I've been pushing myself, more so recently, to just continue to be open and honest because I think it is scary—just the more you grow and the more eyes there are."

The 25-year-old proved she's up for the challenge, having previously pulled back the curtain on her feud with Call Her Daddy's Alex Cooper, opening up about the aftermath of her breakup with Braxton Berrios and sharing insight into testing positive for the BRCA2 gene mutation.

"It's hard to be completely transparent and honest about everything," she reflected. "But at the end of the day, I do really love connecting with people."

"So if anything, I'm trying to just continue to take away the boundaries," the Dancing With the Stars alum said, admitting that "maybe that is not the best, but it's what works for me right now."

But such candor hasn't always come so easily. In fact, Earle didn't dance around the concerns she's had about being so vulnerable on her Netflix series.

"I feel like everything on the show I was so scared about," she admitted. "Even 'til the second we released, I was holding my breath and just like, 'Is this like OK?' You're also reliving moments from six months ago, a year ago, whenever you filmed. It was scary."

But fans also got to see the sweeter side of the Earles—including sister Ashtin Earle, parents Thomas Earle and Alisa Earle, as well as stepparents Ashley Dupré and Todd Galski—as they bickered, bantered and bonded like most families.

"At the end of the day, people coming up to me and being able to relate to something I shared just is the reason I do it all," she added. "And it is cool to have that period of my life captured, all the ups and some downs."

But for now, Earle is relishing one of life's ups: getting styled by Law Roach for the CFDA event at the Museum of Natural History in New York City.

"We decided to go with this because I've never really worn something like this before," she said of her red Isaac Mizrahi gown, adorned with black-beaded florals. "I was obsessed with the color and the beading, and very honored to be wearing it."

And working with Roach—who also styled Brandy and Monica for the annual award show—was tailor-made for Earle.

"It was amazing," she beamed. "I've never worked with him before, but I just met him like a month ago, and this came together last minute, but it was really, really exciting."

"Everything he pulled was so beautiful and so amazing," the influencer continued. "He's very decisive. I'm such an indecisive person, so it was nice to see how he works and puts together a look and his vision."

Un punto más para el Barça en la Youth League (3-3)

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

El Barça se ha enfrentado ante el Aston Villa (3-3) en la cuarta jornada de la fase de liga de la UEFA Youth League. El conjunto azulgrana necesitaba sumar puntos para acercarse a lo alto de la clasificación y evitar la eliminatoria previa a los octavos de final. Tras un partido lleno de intensidad e intentos, han conseguido igualar el marcador gracias a un gol de Iu Martínez en los últimos instantes del encuentro. Los de Pol Planas tienen dos jornadas más por delante para conseguir su objetivo, ante el Sabah y el Manchester City.

El Barça ante el Aston Villa en la Youth League / Sergio Sorolla / @foto.monjo

El Barça salía con todo ante un partido importante con la necesidad de sumar los tres puntos. Pol Planas aprovechó la presencia de Ebrima Tunkara y Hamza Abdelkarim, jugadores con dinámica del primer equipo para echarles una mano desde el inicio del partido. El técnico apostó por un once inicial de gala para reencontrarse con la victoria en Europa tras el empate ante el PSG.

El Barça empezó manteniendo el balón con buenas asociaciones y amplia creatividad para atacar los espacios. Los azulgranas se encontraron con un planteamiento conocido, un rival con falta de ideas sin intención de apretar. Ebrima Tunkara no tardó mucho en demostrar porque habían contado con él para este encuentro. El futbolista azulgrana remató de cabeza con suerte para aprovechar el rechace y abrir el marcador (1-0).

El Aston Villa se vio obligado a reaccionar lo antes posible. El conjunto de James Shan se puso las pilas para tener un par de ocasiones de peligro y acercarse al área rival, pero Bonfill y Baba no cedieron. El conjunto azulgrana se sentía cómodo pero tenía hambre. Tras una buena recuperación, iniciaron un contragolpe efectivo superando rápidamente las zonas del campo hasta que Tunkara encontró a Joni Hernández para ampliar la ventaja en el marcador (2-0).

El Aston Villa despertó tarde. El conjunto inglés apretaba con tanta fuerza en el tramo final de la primer mitad que pudo lograr igualar el marcador con dos saques de esquina (2-2). Los adversarios recortaron la distancia para dirigirse al descanso un poco más aliviados. Sin embargo, una perspectiva muy diferente a los azulgranas que se marchaban encajando dos goles en cuestión de diez minutos.

Giro de 180 grados. Parecía que el Barça tenía las de ganar, pero debían seguir remando. Los azulgranas no agacharon la cabeza, salieron con ganas de llevarse la victoria y con buena presión ofensiva tras la entrada de Gistau y Mirangels. El conjunto azulgrana quería recuperar las sensaciones iniciales para volver a estar cómodos.

La interesante lucha de Tunkara en todos los balones demostraba la mentalidad del equipo: querían ganar. No se daban por vencidos, querían más y necesitaban más. El conjunto de Pol Planas no dejó de generar peligro durante la segunda mitad, los laterales ganaron protagonismo y las jugadas a balón parado eran un incentivo más. Sin embargo, la poca precisión de los disparos les condenaron a optar al empate.

A falta de diez minutos para finalizar el encuentro, el Aston Villa se adelantó al marcador gracias al tanto de Meade (2-3). Los azulgranas mantenían la superioridad sin resultados. El gol encajado les metió más presión hasta que el encuentro dio otro giro, esta vez esperado. Iu Martínez anotó el tercer tanto favorable al conjunto azulgrana para quitarse el mal sabor de boca (3-3). Escasos instantes después, Gistau tuvo en sus pies el gol de la victoria que aún está por ver si entró o no. Con este gol fantasma que sacó la defensa inglesa, el Barça encadenaba un nuevo empate en la competición europea.

Falcons Cut Drake Jackson And Jared Ivey Ahead Of Week 9

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The Atlanta Falcons made a quick roster shakeup on Monday afternoon. The team released outside linebacker Jared Ivey and defensive end Drake Jackson just as Week 9 gets ready to start. This move comes right after a rough 16-0 loss to the Tampa Bay Buccaneers on Sunday. The team needs fresh legs to handle the Cincinnati Bengals next Sunday. Per the Atlanta Falcons official report, both players are gone from the 53-man roster effective immediately.

Ivey played 17 snaps on Sunday but did not record a single stat. He had been on the roster since the team claimed him off waivers in late August. According to the Atlanta Falcons, he appeared in seven games this season with one sack and two tackles for a loss. He started his career with the Seattle Seahawks and was part of their Super Bowl win in 2025. That title makes his release even more surprising for fans who liked his energy. The team let him go despite his recent playing time and past success.

Drake Jackson played 20 snaps over two games before he got cut. He joined the roster on October 24 after spending last year with the Washington Commanders. Per Heavy.com, he was inactive for the game against Tampa Bay on Sunday. His time in Atlanta was short and unproductive. Although he was selected in the second round from USC, staying healthy has proven difficult for him. Injuries and poor play have hurt his career since he was drafted by the 49ers in 2022. The Falcons are clearly looking for someone else to fill the pass-rush role.

The timing of this move points to a specific plan. According to The Falcoholic, James Pearce Jr. is eligible to return from suspension this week. Yasir Abdullah can also come back from injured reserve. These two players offer the speed and run defense the team needs against Joe Burrow. The Falcons likely need the roster spots for these returns. It makes sense to clear the bench for players who have already proven they can play in this system.

The Falcons will try to bring Ivey and Jackson back on the practice squad. Heavy.com notes that Ivey is a Super Bowl champion who might land there quickly. Jackson has the pedigree to get another shot too. The team wants to keep them close in case they need depth later. This is a common move for the NFL when teams need to make room for returning stars. It keeps the talent in the building without using a 53-man spot.

The defense must fix its run issues before Sunday. The team allowed 16 points and no offense against the Buccaneers. A better pass rush is the only way to stop the Bengals offense.

Iraola urges Liverpool to 'kill' Fenerbahce's hostile atmosphere

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Andoni Iraola has urged Liverpool to stop Fenerbahce in Wednesday's Champions League clash by killing the hostile atmosphere generated by their raucous fans.

Iraola is expecting a deafening atmosphere at the Sukru Saracoglu stadium in Istanbul, but the Liverpool boss is confident his side can cope with the unwelcoming environment.

The Spaniard believes Liverpool must cut off the connection between Fenerbahce's players and their passionate fans by taking the sting out of the game.

"Tomorrow, it's not going to be easy. It's always difficult to come to Turkey and play and we have to be very ready," Iraola said on Tuesday.

"They have experienced players who know how to play in this competition. We have to try to kill the confidence and momentum they can have with the supporters.

"Champions League away days are difficult and we know this, especially in this stadium and atmosphere. We have to be mentally very ready.

"We want to continue how we have done in the Champions League. It is a good chance to get to 12 points and then finish in the top eight."

Stopping Fenerbahce's momentum in such a febrile atmosphere might be easier said than done for Liverpool.

In Sunday's 1-0 defeat against Premier League champions Arsenal, Liverpool failed to register a shot on target in a home league game for the first time since records began in 2003.

That lack of cutting edge has been an issue at times for Liverpool this season as they adapt to Iraola's preferred style of play.

But their 100 per cent Champions League record this season has seen them score six against LASK, four against Villarreal and two against Atletico Madrid, compared to 11 in nine Premier League matches.

Asked if he had learnt from Liverpool's impotence against Arsenal, Iraola added: "It is difficult when you lose but honestly I am happy with a lot of things from the other day.

"I liked a lot of things but we still need to improve, especially attacking the box.

"A lot of times we were there with the numbers but not making the proper runs and waiting for second balls.

"We put Arsenal under pressure and they defended well but I liked a lot of things."

Fifth-placed Liverpool are already nine points behind leaders Arsenal in the title race.

It is hardly the start Iraola would have been hoping for in his first season since arriving from Bournemouth to replace the sacked Arne Slot.

"I think we have had better results in the Champions League and Carabao Cup but it's very early in the season to establish comparisons," Iraola said.

Liverpool will be without Dutch midfielder Ryan Gravenberch, who was left at home after reporting discomfort with a heel problem during the warm-up on Sunday.

The Christmas Day schedule is shaping up to be massive

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

When the schedule is released every May, plenty of standalone games that look good at the time morph into something not quite as appetizing as the season unfolds. Some standalone games get better with time.

This year, there’s a trio of massive games lurking on Christmas Day.

At 1:00 p.m. ET, it’s the Packers at the Bears. At 4:30 p.m. ET, it’s the Bills at the Broncos. At 8:15 p.m., it’s the Rams at the Seahawks.

With the Bears emerging as a threat to win the NFC North and the No. 1 seed in the conference, the rematch with their biggest rivals (who beat them 31-7 in Week 5) becomes must-see TV. Bills at Broncos, a rematch of a postseason overtime classic that got Buffalo coach Sean McDermott fired, should have direct implications on two division races and the chase for the lone bye in the AFC.

The nightcap shapes up to be not a battle for the NFC West title and/or the top seed but a potential elimination game, given that both Seattle and L.A. are currently 4-4.

Regardless, all three games should be significant. Impactful. Consequential.

And they’ll more than make up for the Christmas Eve game, which has the 2-5 Texans at the 2-6 Eagles.

Kim Kardashian’s Latest Red Carpet Corset Gown Took 500 Hours to Make

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Kim Kardashian is no stranger to a statement-making corset, but her latest red carpet look required more than 500 hours of craftsmanship.

Ashi Studio designed the gray gown and revealed the lengths its team went to construct the piece on Instagram. “The creation brings together the expertise of multiple artisans, from the intricate construction of the corset and the precise application of lace to the delicate arrangement of each individual cord and the meticulous craftsmanship involved in working with crocodile leather,” it wrote.

Kardashian wore the dress to the New York City premiere of All’s Fair season 2 last night. The strapless design featured a structured bodice with molded cups, a fitted waist, and sheer panels.

Thin cords hung from the sides of the bodice, wrapping loosely around Kardashian’s arms before falling along the length of the skirt. The gown extended into a train, while a matching leather collar covered much of her neck.

Kardashian kept her accessories relatively minimal, opting for diamond stud earrings and a diamond ring. She wore her dark hair in a sleek ponytail with two loose strands framing her face and finished the look with smoky eye makeup and a pale-pink lip.

The fashion house revealed that it first showed Kardashian the gown during a fitting in Los Angeles. The team worked closely with her stylist Law Roach as it completed the design at its Paris atelier.

Roach has been dressing Kardashian throughout the All’s Fair press tour. Just last week, she attended the show’s Rome premiere in a black-and-white polka-dot gown from Galia Lahav’s fall 2027 bridal couture collection.

Kardashian attended Monday’s premiere with her co-stars Sarah Paulson, Naomi Watts, Niecy Nash-Betts, and Glenn Close. The second season of the Ryan Murphy legal drama premieres November 4 on Hulu.

How To Watch Other Mommy At Home

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This has been a great year for horror, from the monumental successes of "Backrooms" and "Obsession," to more high profile favorites like "The End of Oak Street" and "Resident Evil." We've seen independent productions make waves, established filmmakers making their comebacks, and much more. There have also been plenty of lackluster horror movies with just enough interesting ideas to strike a chord with audiences.

Case in point, "Other Mommy," a pretty okay horror film that features Jessica Chastain doing her best Pennywise. That's it, that's the whole movie. Adapting Josh Malerman's novel "Incidents Around the House," Rob Savage helms a stylish horror film that is carried entirely by Jessica Chastain's unhinged performance and a fair amount of violence and spooky stuff.

In spite of less than stellar reviews, "Other Mommy" was a hit with audiences. The movie rose above its abysmal Rotten Tomatoes and CinemaScore evaluation, audiences showed up to theaters, and "Other Mommy" became a box office hit. It's a testament to the marketing department and the trailers for the movie, which hooked audiences on the premise of Savage's film long before the reviews dropped.

Now that Halloween has passed, it's slim pickings for horror at the box office. The good news is that all the horror films released before Halloween are going to start being released on home video soon. Universal Pictures Home Entertainment has announced that "Other Mommy" will release on digital platforms November 10, 2026 with a Blu-ray release expected later, on December 29, 2026.

One of the more intriguing and also confounding aspects of "Other Mommy" is its titular character, whose strange powers seem like Pennywise, Freddy Krueger, and a whole bunch of nightmares combined. It makes her a bit convoluted, sure, but also one of the most exciting horror villains in a while.

It remains to be seen if "Other Mommy" will warrant a sequel, or if Jessica Chastain would return to deliver more horrors. In the meantime, however, you can watch more than what was in theaters, because "Other Mommy" is bringing deleted scenes and other bonus features when it hits home video.

Jessica Chastain stars in a terrifying trip into the darkening world of a young girl, Bela, who forms a relationship with a disturbing entity in her home who looks uncannily like her own mother. 'Other Mommy' proves to be more than a mere figment of the girl's imagination as she becomes a sinister, manipulative presence determined to tear apart Bela's family.

The following bonus content is available when you purchase "Other Mommy" at participating retailers.

EFFECTING FEAR: An exploration of the ways special effects complemented each other to amplify the relentless tension, suspense, and fear driving the story.

A FAMILY IN PIECES: A behind-the-scenes look at the fractured family at the heart of the film and the standout performances that bring them to life.

BREAKING RULES WITH OTHER MOMMY: Cast and filmmakers discuss subverting horror tropes through Bela's perspective and disrupting the expected cadence of the genre.

Medicare's CJR-X Mandate Shifts $392M From Hospitals. ROMTech®'s AI Helps Them Win It Back.

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

ROMTech's award-winning, AI-enabled rehabilitation targets the 90 days after discharge, where CJR-X results are decided.

BROOKFIELD, Conn., Nov. 3, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- On January 1, 2028, Medicare's CJR-X model puts 2,217 hospitals under mandatory two-sided risk for the full cost of hip, knee and ankle replacements through 90 days after discharge. Hospitals that beat a regional target price earn a bonus; hospitals that miss it pay Medicare back.

For most hospitals, the math points to paying back. CMS's final rule projects hospitals will repay $1.855 billion over five years while receiving $1.463 billion, a net $392 million transfer to Medicare. More than half of its projected $736 million in savings comes from pricing alone.

Andy Reid: Chris Jones is giving his all, doing everything he can do

📅 2026-11-03  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Chiefs have lost their last two games.

Coincidentally or not, defensive tackle Chris Jones has been blanked on the official stat sheet in each one.

Jones, who turned 32 in July, has just 13 total tackles with one tackle for loss, three QB hits, and a half-sack through seven games in 2026.

On the whole, the Chiefs have just eight sacks this season, averaging just over one per game. Only Philadelphia has a lower sack rate than Kansas City, which has been getting one on just 3.69 percent of opponent dropbacks. By contrast, Minnesota leads the league with a 12.32 percent sack rate. Chicago is No. 2 at 9.65 percent.

What’s up with the Chiefs’ pass rush? How can the team get things going, particularly with Jones?

“[W]hen you rush four, you want to get somebody home, obviously. That’s what [defensive line coach] Joe [Cullen] strives for, and the guys strive for, and we’ve been doing a good job with getting pressure on,” head coach Andy Reid said in his Monday news conference, via transcript from the team. “You’ve just got to get to the quarterback. We had a lot of blocked balls, which was great, you’ve got to be relatively close to get those. Especially if they’re intermediate throws, the trajectory of the ball is one that you’ve got to be relatively close [to] get a hand up to be able to block.

“But going back to Chris, I mean, Chris is giving his all. He’s doing everything he can do. He gets doubled from various angles. He’s going to draw a lot of attention, and so that’s [an] advantage to the other guys to have a chance to get themselves home. So, as much emphasis is put on Chris, let’s take advantage of that and get ourselves on the quarterback. So, not just to the quarterback, but on the quarterback.”

The Chiefs will be back at home to face the Jets in Week 9. New York ranks No. 26, allowing a sack on 9.62 percent of passing attempts. We’ll see if that can get Kansas City’s pass rush going on Sunday.

'Control Resonant' Becomes a Much-Needed Surprise Hit for Remedy

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Resonant is Remedy's fastest-selling game ever

Fans of Alan Wake and Control creator Remedy Entertainment can finally breathe a sigh of relief. After executive shake-ups and fiscal underperformance at the beloved studio, it seems that the team’s latest game is finding an audience. In a new report released on Tuesday, Remedy wrote that Control Resonant “received excellent player reception and became the fastest-selling Remedy game to date.” The company's third-quarter revenue more than tripled compared to last year, surging by 226%.

What’s more impressive is that the revenue spike includes only Resonant’s pre-orders and its first seven days of sales before the end of the company’s third quarter in late September. The game was released on Sept. 24.

Remedy’s new CEO, Jean-Charles Gaudechon, was quite happy with the game’s performance. “This is our first self-published game of this scale, built on our own Control IP. Praised by players and critics alike, it marks a defining milestone for the company.” Back on Oct. 15, Remedy announced that Resonant had already sold one million copies.

Gaudechon went on to highlight how the game was praised for “Remedy’s willingness to take creative risks and surprise its players.” He added that he was happy with the game’s technical performance. "Positive commentary over its scalability across a variety of PC hardware meant a larger audience could enjoy Control Resonant. This is a testament to our in-house Northlight game engine, which enables the distinctive look and feel that has come to define Remedy’s games.” While Resonant didn't receive unanimous critical praise, reviews were generally positive, albeit not as glowing as the studio's usual output.

Resonant was clearly a big swing for the company, as Gaudechon added that the game received the studio’s largest marketing investment in its history. The United States was its largest market, accounting for nearly half of sales. On PC, China and the U.K. ranked second and third after the U.S., while the U.K. and Canada were second and third for console sales.

Gaudechon expressed satisfaction that the game reached a wider audience. “Based on analytic data, close to 50% of players were new to Control,” he said. “Reaching new players was a key goal for this game, and these early results indicate a growing audience for the franchise and for Remedy.”

In June 2025, Remedy released the multiplayer game FBC: Firebreak, which received fairly tepid reviews and failed to find players. Remedy said the game didn’t meet sales expectations, prompting a profit warning that the company wouldn't hit its fiscal targets for the year. 12 days after the profit warning, former CEO Tero Virtala left his role. Remedy posted a €14.9 million operating loss for 2025.

On Feb. 9, 2026, the company announced that former Electronic Arts executive Gaudechon was the company’s new CEO. While there were concerns that the change would make Remedy resemble the much-hated EA, at least so far, that hasn’t happened. Hopefully, Control Resonant's success means the studio will remain a bastion of strange ideas in a game industry that's increasingly playing it safe.

Alan Wake 2 has been been rescued from The Dark Place.

Europa League, la classifica marcatori: in vetta c'è anche Cisse

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Il sito ufficiale della UEFA riporta la classifica marcatori dell'Europa League 2026-2027 dopo le prime tre giornate della fase campionato: in vetta ci sono sette giocatori a tre gol, tra cui anche l'attaccante del Milan Alphadjo Cisse, autore di una tripletta contro il Salisburgo:

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: Those of you who are retired, how do you like to pay your income taxes?

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We both are retired. We have company pensions, S/S, Interest/Dividend and RMDs incomes. I withhold all of my pension which cover the most of the Federal Taxes due. However, I pay a small portion of money as estimated tax each quarter. I also pay a bit more than estimated State Tax each quarter.

I hope this helps.
Personally I'm leaning towards using a "safe harbor" plan, but if I could use withholding on one or more of my planned sources in income that might be simpler. Unfortunately due to various reasons, some of our income sources will be intermittent and variable so I don't think that idea will work.

Personal Finance (Not Investing) • Re: How to fund new car purchase

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

UPDATE 11/3/26 - Just a quick update on this thread, started over 2 years ago!

We purchased a '26 Honda Pilot last week. We ended up paying ~$27K in cash, with $7K from our brokerage account. The other $25k was financed through Honda at 2.99% for 36 months (total interest is about $1,200). We felt like this was a pretty great rate in the current economy and intend to keep the loan and put any extra cash down on our house mortgage (~5%). Brokerage and retirement accounts have grown nicely over the last two years. So, all in all, I think we are in a good spot.

The car feels like a spaceship compared to our early 2000's fleet. Didn't know a mainstream family SUV would feel like luxury

Konami might have finally set down the Metal Gear Solid Master Collection Vol 1 to focus on releasing Metal Gear Solid 4 from its PS3 prison, but modders continue chipping away at the PS2-and-earlier era Metal Gear games. MGSHDFix, whose mission statement is to repair broken visual bugs, fix crashes, and generally give the older Metal Gears a bit of a spitshine, has got a big new update. Now is the era of MGSHDFix 5.0 (actually, 5.0.1. There was a hotfix).

"We've got performance improvements, massive vanilla loading time reductions for MGS2, loose .MP3/.WAV/.OGG/FLAC soundtrack loading for MG1/MG2, WIP raw mouse input support, and loads of new restoration and bugfixes", writes author Afevis Solmunko.

There are, to be honest, way too many fixes to properly delve into here, so I recommend either checking the patch notes out yourself or taking the mod for a spin in your preferred Metal Gear. We can call out some of the big ones, though.

For example, want to play Metal Gear 2 as Mel Gibson? No? Well that's a shame, bucko, because MGSHDFix 5.0 provides an option to restore that game's MSX2 sprites. "Yes, you can finally experience Mel Gibson fighting Sean Connery as Kojima intended," writes Solmunko.

Also in the OG MGs, there's now an option "to make doors automatically check your inventory for keycards (how MGS2 keycards work)", which frankly sounds blasphemous to me.

More practically, the latest version of the mod boasts that it dramatically cuts down loading times in MGS2. "We've greatly sped up level loading by adding a new system that prefetches a list of all the assets for all the rooms that are directly connected to the current stage you're in… Speedrunners have reported that this new prefetch system has shaved upwards to 5 minutes off of the elapsed realtime from their runs". On Linux (and thus, Steam Deck) specifically, Solmunko says this can "reduce level transitions load times by 4~6 seconds for every room you go into".

Plus, MGS2 and MGS3 now have limited support for raw mouse input in third-person, with support for first-person hopefully coming in MGSHDFix 5.1.0.

All good news, in other words, and it's nice these classics are now released in a way that lets fans do this kind of loving work for as long as they like.

25 Years Later, Gandalf's Wisest Lord Of The Rings Quote Has No Equal In Fantasy Movie History

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

25 years later, Gandalf the Grey's words of wisdom in The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of the Ring still hold true. If Samwise Gamgee is the beating heart of the Fellowship, and Frodo Baggins its soul, then Gandalf is its mind, the leader who understands that knowledge is just as great a power as any magical ability he possesses, and perhaps even more so.

There are a lot of great Gandalf lines from throughout the three Lord of the Rings movies (and, of course, J.R.R. Tolkien's books long before them), which help to make him one of the most beloved characters. "A wizard is never late..." is iconic, and his lines in the scene with the Balrog - "You shall not pass!" and "Fly, you fools," - have been endlessly quoted ever since The Fellowship of the Ring hit theaters in 2001.

But as memorable as they are, there is one that stands out from the rest as being the greatest words of wisdom imparted by the wizard, which not only shapes the entire narrative, but applies directly to all of our own lives, too.

The Gandalf quote in question comes when Frodo laments the heavy burden the One Ring has placed upon him, wishing it had not come to him, and that none of it had happened. To this, the grey wizard says:

"So do all who live to see such times, but that is not for them to decide. All we have to decide is what to do with the time that is given to us."

The line works on multiple levels. To Frodo, it is exactly what he needs to hear at that moment, even if it's not necessarily what he might want to hear. Gandalf offers him a sense of comfort, in that the Hobbit is not alone in his thoughts, validating his wishes. But at the same time, he doesn't pander to him and tell him that it's going to be easy, but does state his own sense of agency in these events.

This speaks to the Fellowship at large, and Middle-earth as a whole. It's a perfect way of distilling down the ideas of free will vs. what's already divined that are at play in The Lord of the Rings, which we also get a sense of in the same scene, as Gandalf tells Frodo there are other forces at play, and that: "Bilbo was meant to find the ring. In which case, you also were meant to have it."

That juxtaposition of those quotes might suggest a conflict between free will and fate or luck in this story, but that's not actually the case. We do know that fate exists in Middle-earth, via the creator of Arda, Erū Ilúvatar, but they also granted people free will. And we see time and again throughout the story that, while fate and luck play their hands, it's the choices people make that ultimately define the story.

This is clear with all the Fellowship of the Ring members, each choosing to follow Frodo ("my sword... my bow... my ax..."), and all being tested at various turns, and the outcomes are entirely dependent on which path they choose. Fate may have played a part in Frodo getting the ring, but it was, as Gandalf says, deciding what to do, such as Sam being by his side, or Frodo not killing Gollum, that all led to the One Ring being destroyed.

All of this is enough to make Gandalf's quote one of the most powerful in fantasy history, but it's just how relevant and relatable it is — whether it's 1954, 2001, or 2026 — that makes it even better. Because what Gandalf is really getting at is a core tenet of humanity and how we live our lives. None of us could choose when we were born, nor the surrounding circumstances, but all we can do is decide what to do with the time given to us. It's a simple, beautiful message that continues to resonate far beyond The Lord of the Rings.

That Gandalf line alone is incredible, but what's just as impressive is that we get another bit of profound wisdom in the same scene. When Frodo talks about wishing Bilbo had killed Gollum when he had the chance, the wizard responds:

"Many that live deserve death, and some that die deserve life. Can you give it to them, Frodo? Do not be too eager to deal out death in judgment. Even the very wise cannot see all ends."

Again, it's a quite simple yet elegant and fitting way of boiling down something that is so fundamentally human. It's easy to condone, but Gandalf here teaches an important lesson about humility, morality, and judgment that also endures, and connects perfectly to his line about time. After all, it is Frodo remembering these words, and sparing Gollum's life, that becomes central to the whole story, and thus free will and fate entwine together, as highlighted by Gandalf's classic The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of The Ring quotes.

The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of the Ring is the first film in Peter Jackson's critically acclaimed Lord of the Rings trilogy. The movie follows Frodo Baggins as he is tasked with destroying the One Ring in the fires of Mount Doom after he inherits the ring.

Believe It Or Not, Mr. Ripley Gets Meta in 'A Talent for Murder' Origin | Review

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

You're likely much more familiar with Tom Ripley — the sociopathic social climber who murdered his way across high society in a series of novels, films and shows including 1999's The Talented Mr. Ripley and Netflix's recent Ripley — than you are with his creator, cantankerous crime novelist Patricia Highsmith. But in the new meta-thriller A Talent for Murder, director Anton Corbijn and screenwriter Joanna Murray-Smith make a bet that the author herself is just as fascinating and shocking as her most famous character. And they might be right — at least, when Helen Mirren is playing the aggressive, provocative and famously bigoted Highsmith, in a swaggering performance that lets the typically elegant actress cut loose.

Mirren's Highsmith is the heart of a story that's one-half eccentric literary biopic, one-half pulpy mystery: what if Highsmith, in her final days, was approached by a mysterious young editor begging her for one final book? The tale that unspools from there never quite becomes the sleek cat-and-mouse thriller it would like to be. But it is a lot of fun, and a risky third act twist transforms the film from a typical mystery into a commentary about the nature of life as an artist — one that will leave viewers either elated or enraged.

In early 1995, 74-year-old Patricia Highsmith (Mirren) is living in solitude at her isolated Swiss country home, indulging in her favorite pastimes: guzzling booze, reminiscing on her professional triumphs, terrorizing the townsfolk with her bad temper, and enjoying the company of her large collection of knives, poisons, and guns. Enter Edward Ridgeway (Alden Ehrenreich), a young book editor who has been sent all the way from New York City with a single mission: get Highsmith to sign the contract for a sixth Tom Ripley novel.

Ridgeway is a Highsmith fanatic, and knows that getting her back to work would make his career. But he's also not quite comfortable with the situation he's been dropped into — or the mysterious fate of a previous editor, who had also been sent to extract a new book from Highsmith, but soon vanished off the face of the earth. Without an easy way back home, he tries to make his case. Of course, the irascible author tries to send him packing, using colorful language and some threats to his life. But even as the two clash, Highsmith is soon seduced by the idea that there's still demand for her work, that a final book could cement her literary immortality, and that Ridgeway might be the one to pull her out of the writer's block that has swallowed her final years.

From there, the story of Highsmith and Ridgeway's relationship weaves in and out of the fictional tale they spin, as they begin work together on her next Ripley novel. In scenes illustrating their story ideas, Ripley (also played by Ehrenreich) is determined to marry a young American named Clementine (Olivia Cooke) — an heiress who happens to share a name, face, and life story with an actual young woman Ridgeway met on his way to Switzerland. As real and imagined storylines get increasingly tangled, it becomes difficult to tell what's true and what's just a story. What is Ridgeway's real interest in Clementine? What is Ridgeway's real interest in Highsmith, for that matter? Who's actually manipulating who? And what's so damned important about Tom Ripley?

A Talent for Murder's greatest shock isn't any of the crimes that occur on screen — it's watching Mirren, an actor known for maintaining an air of poise even in her more villainous roles, play a character who is rude, sloppy and purposely indelicate. Sporting a head of barely-brushed hair and a confrontational sneer, Highsmith is delighted by her own crassness, whether she's holding forth on the mating habits of snails, speculating on Ridgeway's own sexual prowess, or just casually fondling some items from her firearm collection. Highsmith is a nightmare, but an engrossing one — and through monologues that reveal more than she intends about her past, we learn exactly how she developed such a rough shell, and how those difficult experiences also led her to create her charming criminal mastermind. Mirren is clearly having a ball playing a complete asshole, which makes her antics a joy to watch — and reveals that even at 81, there are still sides we have yet to see of the actress.

Ehrenreich doesn't undergo quite as big a transformation, but viewers who are only familiar with him from his time as Han Solo will be shocked by his turn as the nebbishy Ridgeway, who swings between playing the innocent and revealing hints of a more threatening core. While he isn't always compelling on his own, he and Mirren fight beautifully; his seemingly inexpert attempts to manipulate her and her over-the-top diatribes about him bring to mind a platonic version of Who's Afraid of Virginia Woolf? Their blackly comedic attempts to take each other down allow Murray-Smith to slip in grand statements about art, creativity, and the nature of good and evil in a way that's genuinely entertaining — you'll be so tickled watching Mirren suggest that she poisoned Ehrenreich's eggs, you won't even notice when the conversation pivots to the value of art or the greater meaning of a painful childhood.

Ehrenreich's scenes as Ripley, which mostly feature narration from Highsmith and Ridgeway in place of dialogue, see the actor take the killer to a lighter, campier place than Matt Damon, Andrew Scott, or others who have tackled the role; he glories in the character's external sleaze but skips the inner torment, a decision that makes these sections pure fun. This is especially true when he plays off Cooke, who makes her character such a wide-eyed cartoon of naïveté that you don't really feel much sorrow over her constant misfortune; instead, you just get caught up in the enjoyable nastiness of Ripley's scheming.

However, as A Talent for Murder enters its final act, you may begin to wonder why exactly you're watching these two plot threads play out. It's lovely to be out there getting up to no good in the snow-covered Alps or sunny Roman piazzas (which are captured in luscious cinematography by Robbie Ryan), but what is all this plotting and stalking and mysterious phone call-making adding up to?? A few moments of confusion towards the film's end culminate in an audacious climax that viewers will either love or hate — a reaction that honestly feels much more in line with Highsmith's legacy as a person and writer than any traditional mystery movie denouement. The film's decision to make a big statement on the nature of art and artists feels slightly out of left field when it arrives, but it's also far more meaningful than any possible whodunnit reveal. You'll likely be thinking about it days later — though whether that's because you loved it or because you want your money back is impossible to predict.

A Talent for Murder began as a stage play (also written by Murray-Smith), a pedigree that's fairly obvious in the way it plays out — there are rarely more than two characters on screen at any given time, and they are prone to occasionally making grand philosophical declarations in the way only theater characters seem to.

Its beginnings as a play are also clear in the way the film values character over plot. Though some of the twists and turns don't quite work out, the emotional battle between the protagonists always feels right. Highsmith and Ridgeway's fights are funny, frightening, and strange, but more than anything, they reveal that the things we scorn and the things we need are often more similar than we'd like to admit. Of course, noting that there's a thin line between love and hate isn't exactly a revolutionary idea. But I can almost guarantee, you've never seen anyone illustrate it the way A Talent for Murder does.

An unconventional biopic that isn't afraid to go to strange places.

Apple Just Stockpiled $10 Billion of Parts While Warning Memory Prices Are 'Unprecedented.' Should Investors Worry?

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Apple's annual report shows it loaded up on components as memory costs soar. Here's what that means for its margins, and for the stock.

Apple (AAPL -2.08%) just revealed to investors that it's buying parts as fast as it can, and it is paying up big to do it.

The company's annual report, filed Tuesday, shows components inventory of $10.4 billion as of Sept. 26, up from $2.1 billion a year earlier. Meanwhile, finished goods, meaning iPhones and Macs sitting in warehouses, barely moved, rising to $4.1 billion from $3.6 billion. In other words, nearly all of the buildup in inventories is parts.

The filing came a day after John Ternus, on his first earnings call as CEO, said "What we're seeing with memory pricing right now is unprecedented" and warned that costs will climb further. Put the two together, and you get a clear picture of how the world's most valuable consumer company is handling a memory shortage.

The parts pile isn't the only sign. Apple had $89.7 billion in manufacturing purchase obligations at fiscal year-end, with $82.7 billion due within 12 months. A year earlier, that total was $56.2 billion. Ternus said those obligations rose by $33 billion in the September quarter alone.

Building that stockpile cost real cash. Inventory soaked up $8.9 billion of operating cash flow in fiscal 2026, after adding $1.4 billion the year before. Of course, Apple can afford it: The tech giant still generated $145.1 billion in cash from operations, and it spent $85.8 billion buying back its own stock.

The 10-K is blunt about why. Apple says it is facing supply constraints and rising costs for advanced chips, memory, and storage, and it "expects these trends to intensify for memory and storage components." It also warns that price increases "may not effectively mitigate these negative impacts, and may also reduce demand for the Company's products."

The cost is already hitting margins. Apple's gross margin was 47.9% in the September quarter, but that included about one percentage point from tariff refunds. Without those refunds, CFO Kevin Parekh said, margin fell about a point from the June quarter, "driven entirely by memory impacts."

The December quarter looks tighter. Parekh guided gross margin to 45%-46%, mainly due to higher memory costs and some spending to roll out Siri AI. Ternus added that memory prices should keep rising beyond December, while the cushion from cheaper parts Apple bought earlier shrinks.

Apple has already passed some of that on to customers. Parekh said the company "reluctantly" raised prices on iPad and Mac around June and on iPhone in September, and it's too early to say how buyers will respond.

Memory is also limiting what Apple can sell. Chief Operating Officer Sabih Khan said Mac and iPad supply in the December quarter will be held back mainly by memory constraints, as demand has run ahead of expectations.

Despite the frenzy around memory, there's one big bright spot for Apple: Product demand is certainly not a problem. Revenue rose 12% to a record $114.5 billion for the September quarter, and iPhone sales jumped 17% to $57.6 billion. Meanwhile, services grew 10% to $31.6 billion. For the full year, revenue climbed 15% to $478.8 billion and net income reached $132.2 billion.

Apple also expects December-quarter revenue to grow 7% to 10%, which Parekh said would make it the biggest revenue quarter in company history.

And the stockpile cuts both ways. When memory is scarce, the company that can commit tens of billions of dollars to suppliers in advance gets served first. Smaller phone and PC makers don't have Apple's balance sheet, so the same shortage that trims Apple's margin could cost them sales outright.

The memory squeeze is also a signal about who's winning right now. When Apple, one of the largest buyers of memory chips in the world, says prices are "unprecedented," that's good news for the semiconductor companies selling them, including Micron Technology (MU +2.32%).

For Apple shareholders, an important number to track is gross margin. If it stays near the 45% to 46% range and revenue keeps growing, the memory hit is a speed bump. If price increases start to dent iPhone demand, as Apple's own filing warns they could, that's a bigger problem.

So, is Apple stock a buy? The company is choosing to protect supply and sales over a point or two of margin, and it has the cash to do it while still paying a $0.27 quarterly dividend and buying back stock. For long-term investors, that looks like the right trade. Just don't expect margins to look as clean as they did last year until memory prices cool down.

Jonathan Ponciano has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Apple and Micron Technology. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Apple Stock Fell After a Record iPhone Quarter. Here’s the Margin Problem Investors Shouldn’t Ignore.

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Investors are looking past iPhone growth to how much profit Apple keeps on each device, and rising memory costs are pushing that down.

Apple (AAPL -2.08%) just delivered the biggest September quarter in its history, with revenue jumping 12% to $114.5 billion. Its iPhone business performed even better, and earnings per share topped Wall Street's expectations.

So why did Apple stock fall about 2% on Tuesday?

The answer has less to do with how many iPhones Apple is selling and more to do with how much money it's making on each one. Rising memory chip prices are putting pressure on Apple's profit margins, and management doesn't expect relief anytime soon.

I'm still bullish on Apple, but investors shouldn't dismiss this problem.

Apple's iPhone revenue jumped 17.5% year over year to $57.6 billion, beating Wall Street's estimate of $56.5 billion. That's an outstanding result for a product line that already generates more than $200 billion in annual sales.

Not everything went Apple's way. Its iPad revenue declined 10%, while wearables revenue fell 8%. Management attributed those declines partly to difficult comparisons with last year's product launches and to supply constraints affecting the iPad.

But the iPhone remains Apple's biggest business, and its performance suggests consumers are still willing to pay a premium for the company's flagship products. The problem is that Apple is paying more to make them.

Apple reported a gross margin of 47.9% for its fiscal fourth quarter of 2026 (ending Sept. 26), although that figure benefited from roughly 1 percentage point of tariff refunds. Without those refunds, its gross margin would have been approximately 46.9%.

For the December quarter, management expects gross margins between 45% and 46%.

That might not sound like much of a difference. But every percentage point of gross margin on $114.5 billion in revenue represents roughly $1.1 billion in gross profit. And Apple expects its December quarter to be even bigger.

The culprit compressing margins is memory chips, which are becoming increasingly expensive as demand for artificial intelligence infrastructure puts pressure on supply. CEO John Ternus described memory pricing as "unprecedented," while CFO Kevan Parekh said rising memory costs accounted for the entire underlying gross margin decline between the June and September quarters.

Apple has already raised prices on its iPhones, Macs, and iPads, but it's also benefiting from memory purchased earlier at lower prices. As those inventories are depleted, higher costs will increasingly affect profitability.

And there's another complication. Apple stock trades at roughly 35 times expected earnings, leaving less room for disappointing results.

I don't think rising memory prices fundamentally change Apple's long-term investment thesis.

For starters, Apple's services business carries a gross margin of approximately 76%, making it substantially more profitable than hardware sales. That business provides an important cushion against rising manufacturing costs, although management expects services revenue growth to slow to between 8% and 9% in the December quarter.

Apple also has the financial resources and purchasing power to secure memory supplies. Its manufacturing purchase obligations increased by $33 billion during the quarter, reflecting efforts to lock in future production.

That doesn't eliminate the margin problem, and Apple's Siri AI rollout will introduce additional costs. But memory prices are cyclical, and Apple has weathered component shortages and rising manufacturing expenses before. The bigger question is how much of those costs Apple can pass along to customers without hurting demand.

When Apple reports its Q1 2027 results, I'll be watching whether gross margins land within management's 45% to 46% forecast. I'll also be paying attention to services growth and whether supply constraints affecting Macs and iPads begin to ease.

Apple's iPhone business is performing exceptionally well, and I don't see any reason to abandon my bullish outlook over one disappointing margin forecast.

But at 35 times expected earnings, Apple needs more than strong sales. It needs to demonstrate that it can keep those sales profitable, even as the cost of producing its products increases.

Patrick Sanders has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Apple. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Astronomers found the first star that would look green to the human eye

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Science  |  Read Source →

Astronomers have identified the first known green star, a white dwarf tinted by sodium from planetary debris, alongside unusually colored stars.

A dead star called WD J1330+6435 is the first known star that would appear green to human eyes. Astronomers at the University of Warwick identified it while working out the true colors of nearby white dwarfs.

The star’s green tint comes from sodium in rocky material it has pulled in from its own planetary system. That sodium absorbs the orange part of the star’s light.

The same work flagged WD J1011+2845, just 48 light-years from Earth, as what may be the reddest star ever recorded.

White dwarfs are the dense remnants left behind when Sun-like stars reach the end of their lives. None is close enough for its color to be seen directly.

The researchers worked from each star’s spectrum, the pattern produced when starlight is split apart by wavelength, and ran those spectra through a model of human color vision.

The sample covered the 151 white dwarfs within 20 parsecs, or about 65 light-years, of Earth.

The results correct a common misconception in astronomy that white dwarfs are typically blue. Hotter, bluer white dwarfs are brighter, so surveys based on brightness alone over-represent them.

Dr. Mark Hollands, a Warwick Prize Fellow in the Astronomy and Astrophysics Group at the university, led the work.

“Most stars, including most white dwarfs, behave like ‘perfect radiators,’ so their colour is set almost entirely by temperature,” Hollands said.

“It turns out these incredibly dense stars can produce colours nobody expected, and some of them genuinely surprised me,” he added.

WD J1330+6435 has a temperature of roughly 7,200°F (4,000°C). It has accreted rocky material from its planetary system, enriching its atmosphere with calcium, lithium and sodium.

The atmospheres of these stars can reach roughly the density of water, millions of times denser than the Sun’s visible surface, the university explained.

Because this star’s atmosphere is so dense, the sodium absorbs light across a broad range of yellow, orange and red wavelengths. What remains gives the star a chartreuse green appearance in the team’s models.

As far as the researchers are aware, no other star, living or dead, has ever been credibly reported as green. That makes WD J1330+6435 the first known genuinely green star in the Galaxy.

“Imagine standing on a planet and looking up at a green star,” Hollands noted. “It feels like something out of science fiction.”

White dwarfs that pull in the remains of planets carry elements such as lithium, sodium and calcium in their atmospheres, where astronomers can read them in starlight.

In 2021, Hollands and his colleagues reported lithium in four cool, old white dwarfs with cooling ages of five to ten billion years. WD J1330+6435, catalogued then as SDSS J1330+6435, was one of the four.

That work placed the star about 87 parsecs, or roughly 280 light-years, from Earth.

The amounts of lithium and potassium compared with sodium and calcium “strongly suggest that all four white dwarfs have accreted fragments of planetary crusts,” the authors wrote in Nature Astronomy.

Another star in that set, WD J2317+1830, also showed extra infrared light, a sign that it was still drawing material from a surrounding disk of dust and rock.

In the new color study, white dwarfs enriched with fragments of shattered asteroids and comets tend toward yellow. Carbon-rich white dwarfs are typically pink or purple.

In WD J0902+2010, the convection zone has reached the core and lifted interior carbon to the surface. There, carbon forms C2 molecules that absorb blue, green and yellow light, so the star would look pink to the human eye.

WD J1011+2845 goes further. Carbon molecules distort its light so heavily that almost all of it is squeezed into wavelengths beyond 600 nanometers, producing a deep salmon-pink glow.

Cooler white dwarfs with mixed hydrogen and helium atmospheres often take on a cyan or pale blue tinge. That color comes from hydrogen molecules colliding with helium atoms in their ultra-dense atmospheres.

Sirius B, the nearest white dwarf to the Solar System, has a hot hydrogen atmosphere that gives it a bluish color in the models.

Of the 151 nearby white dwarfs, five showed unusual coloring: two yellow and three pink. That suggests roughly one in 30 white dwarfs in the Universe shines with an unusually colored light.

For the remaining 146 stars, the median color temperature was about 6,800 kelvin, roughly 11,800°F (6,530°C), which people perceive as white.

Professor Boris Gänsicke of the Department of Physics at Warwick co-authored the study.

“One in thirty might not sound like a huge fraction, but across the entire population of white dwarfs in the Universe, that is still a lot of oddly coloured stars out there,” Gänsicke said.

“Yet if we take the median across a representative population of white dwarfs, we found that the average white dwarf really is white,” he explained.

The study was published in the journal RAS Techniques and Instruments.

Lens, mister Cahuzac: "Fabregas per me fonte d'ispirazione. Como? Non faccio calcoli"

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Dopo un brutto ko nel derby contro il Lille di Davide Ancelotti, il Lens intende rialzare la testa e ripartire subito, per ovviare anche ai recenti risultati negativi maturati in Champions League. Solo una vittoria - contro lo Slavia Praga - poi ko con Sporting Lisbona e Club Brugge, domani sera al Bollaert-Delelis invece ospiterà il Como di Cesc Fabregas con ben 6 punti in tre turni in League Phase, al debutto assoluto nella competizione. L'allenatore dei Sang et Or, Yannick Cahuzac, ha parlato in conferenza stampa alla vigilia dell'incontro.

Prima di tutto il punto sulla squadra: "Odsonne Édouard è rientrato dal suo intervento. Maik Nawrocki, Ismaelo Ganiou e Abdallah Sima stanno proseguendo il loro percorso di riatletizzazione. Per il resto, ho a disposizione tutto il rimanente gruppo", ha informato il tecnico di 41 anni. Di certo si aspetterà un impianto bollente domani, per riportare il Lens alla vittoria e ritrovare punti in Europa: "Ci aspettiamo di avere il pubblico dalla nostra parte. Sappiamo di avere un pubblico fantastico che ci sosterrà, soprattutto se metteremo in campo le cose giuste. Sta a noi agire per trascinarli con noi".

Infine, un pensiero sull'avversario che sfiderà domani, reduce da un filotto di cinque vittorie complessive di fila e un ottimo momento di forma. Al netto di cinque defezioni importanti: "Non faccio calcoli", ha detto Cahuzac. "È una grande squadra con un vero piano di gioco, molto efficiente nel possesso palla. Mettono inoltre molta mobilità a centrocampo e sono molto interessanti nell'utilizzo e nella conquista degli spazi. È una squadra ben collaudata nei movimenti, una formazione molto completa. Cesc Fabregas è un allenatore che per me rappresenta una fonte d'ispirazione. È giovane ma ha idee vere".

Fontaines D.C. continues its momentum on Billboard’s charts. World of Warcraft Forever release countdown: Exact date and time

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

World of Warcraft: Forever releases soon, and it reimagines 2004's World of Warcraft release with improved graphics and overhauled gameplay.

World of Warcraft: Forever takes us to a new version of Azeroth back when everything began, but this time reimagined as something both familiar and curiously different.

Introduced as a completely separate experience from Retail and Classic, this new World of Warcraft promises a return to what made the original 2004 MMO an icon in gaming. We already got a taste of our new adventures in the beta, and now the rest of the world can retake their first steps in Azeroth when World of Warcraft: Forever finally releases. Here’s when the game goes live.

WoW: Forever goes live on Wednesday, Nov. 4. Blizzard already announced this release date, with reservations for character names opened on Oct. 27 (and ending today, Nov. 4!). It’s very doubtful that Blizzard will make a sudden change to the release schedule. This countdown will expire when Blizzard launches the game, and we’ll be well on our way to Azeroth.

The countdown is based on the following times:

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

WoW: Forever reimagines the original WoW with a new overarching ethos: adventure is forever. Return to Azeroth as it appeared in WoW’s 2004 launch, but this time revamped as a completely new experience outside the intensity of modern Retail and the nostalgic Classic lines. No Outlands or alternate timelines (for now) – just Azeroth rebuilt with a far larger horizon.

While built with the framework of the Vanilla launch, WoW: Forever boasts improved graphics with your choice of standard and high-definition models, as well as atmospheric and immersive lighting. These fit right up the alley of the game’s reimagined zones and new destinations (like Zephras Isle, Shen’dralas, Riverglades, and Mount Hyjal), which are some of the sites of Forever‘s 1,000+ new quests.

WoW: Forever comes with the arrival of the Skyborne Elves, or the Shen’dorei, who took up residence on Zephras Isle in the Elemental Plane of Air. They vanished from the face of Azeroth since the Sundering in the First War, having only resurfaced after thousands of years. The Skyborne now share an ancestry deeply rooted in the air and whatever’s left of their ancient Kaldorei culture – something far different from their Night Elf cousins.

Built with the broader Warcraft audience in mind, Forever offers a little something for everyone. Instead of realms, you now make a character based on a specific ruleset, whether it’s traditional, PVP-focused, roleplay-centric, or RP with PVP. You can also choose a unique last name exclusive to your region, while the new Legacy system offers account-wide rewards when leveling up different characters.

Gameplay still offers the original nine Classes, but this time offering new combinations (and lore) with other Races. Classes and Professions have been revamped to be more flexible and useful throughout leveling, and the Camping system aims to foster a culture of camaraderie among adventurers. WoW: Forever also features a revamped UI, improved accessibility features, and complete controller support.
